## The approved owner plan (verbatim, .feature/plan-owner.md)

# The plan: the whole product in one feature

## What happens

1. Landing. A visitor lands on oparax.ai. The page explains what Oparax does, how the alerts work and what it costs, shows the owner's own agent as a real example, and carries the box: an X handle and one sentence, the beat.
2. Building. On submit, oparax.ai/<handle> opens in its building state and shows the three steps as they happen: looking the person up on X, reading their newest posts, choosing sources and X accounts. Onboarding ends by writing a detailed brief on the person, saved once. Progress is saved as it goes, so closing the tab never starts a second build; reopening the page shows where it is. Typing a handle that already has a page opens that page.
3. The agent page. The person's picture and name, the beat, the brief, up to ten sites and feeds with one sentence each on why they fit, at least five X accounts, and the feed. The feed starts at once with each source's ten newest items from the last two days. New articles keep arriving on their own.
4. The feed. Every new article is judged against the beat with the brief in view. Articles that fit become cards: a headline and one to five facts, each with its quote from the article, checked against the article. Reports of one story fold into one story card listing its sources. Two views, single articles and stories, and a "skipped" list of what was judged off the beat, with its score. Posts from the watched X accounts go through the same judging, grouping and cards, with the account's handle and name on the card.
5. The story link. Each story has its own address; opening it shows the page with that card in view: headline, facts with their sources, the publishers, the time, an image when a source had one. This is what the bot links to.
6. The bot. A button opens the person's own X composer addressed to @oparax_ai with a one-time code. Sending it from the X account the page belongs to connects the two; a message from any other account is ignored. From then on the bot sends one DM per story, ever: headline, first fact, the story link. Hobby and Creator get one DM a day at 2 pm their local time; Wire gets a mini digest every 15 minutes when there is news. STOP, PAUSE and RESUME as replies do what they say.
7. The free week. The first view in a real browser by anyone but the owner starts the clock; the owner's own visits, automatic link previews and bots never do. The page then shows the price and the days left. During the week the recommended X accounts are watched, up to 300 posts. The page is read-only for everyone until it is paid for. A page nobody views pauses after 14 days and restarts on its first view.
8. Day seven. The page freezes: old cards stay readable, nothing new arrives, and the page shows the three prices: Hobby $5, Creator $30, Wire $99 a month. Paying through Stripe checkout is the sign-up: the email typed at checkout creates or finds the account, binds the page to it, and unfreezes it. The person gets a sign-in link by email. No card before day seven.
9. Sign-in. The login page offers email, Continue with Google and Continue with X. A signed-in person whose email has no agent sees the landing box and sets one up.
10. Settings, after paying. Add or remove sources; mark a source "do not filter"; pick which X accounts to watch, each showing its posts per day and the running total against the monthly pool, with one warning when an account alone would empty the pool in under ten days; set the DM hour; switch on the GitHub and Product Hunt digests; manage the subscription and cancel through Stripe's portal. When the pool hits zero the accounts pause until the month resets, sites continue, and the page says so.
11. Digests. With GitHub on, code turns the beat into a few topics, searches GitHub daily for new repos past 100 stars or rising fast, has Jev judge each against the beat, and shows the fits as cards: name, a why-now line, the repo's own description. The person can name repos they follow and a star threshold (3,000 by default); a card appears when one passes it. Product Hunt is the same shape with its daily launches.
12. Public pages. The privacy and terms text says what the product now does. The Contact dialog takes an email and a message, stores it and emails no-reply@oparax.ai.

## What happens when it fails

- The handle does not exist on X: "Handle not found" under the box. X itself unavailable: the page says the lookup could not finish and offers to try again.
- A build stops partway: the page shows which step failed and a "Try again" that resumes the saved work; only the extra work is charged and the page's reservation stays.
- The $200 is used up: the box says builds are unavailable for now and offers to leave a handle; nothing promises a reset.
- A source cannot be read: the page marks it "could not read its last N items"; a readable item clears the mark. No card is ever written from a teaser.
- A fact fails a check: the writer gets one repair pass; what still fails is withheld, never shown. With no fact left, the page shows the article's title as an unverified report with its link and sends no DM. A failed rewrite keeps the previous checked card.
- A judgment or write fails on the model's side: the item stays pending and is retried on the next run; the page tells "no relevant news yet" apart from "could not process".
- The bot cannot deliver: the story stays on the page, is marked undelivered for the owner, and is never sent twice. A send whose outcome is unknown is held for the owner, not retried.
- The pool is empty: the accounts pause, the page says so, sites continue.
- Renewal payment fails: the feed runs until the paid-through date, then freezes with "payment failed, update your card" and a link to the Stripe portal; a later successful payment unfreezes it.
- Payment confirmed but the account not yet connected: the return page says the account is being connected and to check the email; nothing paid is lost.
- A sign-in link has expired: the login page offers a new one.
- The first view's location is unknown: the DM hour defaults to 2 pm UTC until the person sets it.
- A source spends more than $1 in a day: it is paused, marked on the page and in the operational record; a busy but cheap source is never touched.
- X credits low (under $15 by our own count) or the daily balance read fails: new anonymous builds and free-week X polling pause; paying users keep running; the owner sees the last known balance.

## The decisions

- Design source: stock shadcn Mira per DESIGN.md, so every screen looks like the rest of the site today, and the owner restyles pages in Claude Design later, per page (owner, September 28).
- The person-facing name is "agent", one per person, so screens say "your Oparax agent", because that is the marketing angle (owner, September 28).
- Three tiers, Hobby $5 with 100 watched posts a month and a daily DM, Creator $30 with 3,000 and a daily DM, Wire $99 with 4,000 and a digest every 15 minutes, sites and feeds unlimited, so watched X posts are the only metered thing, because X bills half a cent per post and everything else is nearly free (owner, September 28; $99 and the pools the assistant's recommendation he accepted).
- The free week starts at the first outside view, carries 300 watched posts, pauses unvisited pages after 14 days and freezes on day seven with the prices, so nobody claims a page and no card is asked for early, because the product should be seen working before anyone pays (owner, September 28).
- $200 in total for builds without sign-up, $3 reserved per build, so about 66 pages can be started before the box closes, because a page's cost arrives over its week, not at build time (owner, September 28: "$200 total", correcting the brief's "a day"; the $3 is the assistant's arithmetic from the cost file: onboarding, 300 posts, seven DMs, three weeks of judging).
- Sites and feeds are checked every minute (owner, September 28), so discovery is timely, because checks are free; a story appears after it has been read, judged and checked, not within the minute. Watched X accounts every minute for Wire and every five minutes otherwise, because X allows the whole app 10,000 requests per 15 minutes and every-minute polling would hit that at about 666 accounts (owner, September 28, with the reason recorded; the 666 corrects the 150 said tonight).
- The daily DM hour defaults to 2 pm in the person's local time from their first view's location, changeable after paying, so the DM arrives in their afternoon, because X tells us no timezone (owner, September 28).
- One DM per story, ever, at the tier's cadence, with the story link; later reports improve the card and never DM again; no instant alerts (owner, September 28). A send with an unknown outcome is held for the owner rather than retried, so nobody gets a story twice (assistant's default).
- The bot connects only when the message comes from the X account the page was built for, so a stranger cannot bind someone's page to their own inbox (assistant's default). The bot sends with its stored token, proven tonight by a real DM to the owner (owner-authorized, September 28); receiving messages needs X's webhook, registered once the site is deployed, and is not proven by that send.
- Watched X posts go through the same pipeline as articles, judged, grouped, carded and DM'd, with the account's handle and name, so the person sees one feed, because that is how the earlier product worked (owner, September 28).
- Onboarding ends with a detailed brief on the person, saved once; Jev reads it in every judgment and Qwen in every card; each source carries onboarding's sentence on why it fits and Jev reads that too, so judgments follow the person, not only the beat sentence (owner, September 28). The brief is shown on the person's page (assistant's default).
- The profile request is trimmed to what code uses plus the picture and the affiliation lookup is dropped, so nothing unused is paid for (owner, September 28).
- Judging: fit at 0.5 on, under 0.35 off, unsure off (owner, September 28); grouping at 0.75 within a 72-hour window and additions at 0.75 (assistant's defaults); Qwen 3.7 Flash writes, Jev checks support, attribution and the headline at 0.5, one repair pass (owner, September 21 to 28); day zero is ten items per source from the last two days (owner, September 26). Both views are written, one card per fitting item and one per story, so the person chooses how to read (owner, September 22); that is two writes per grouped item, cents a month.
- Payment: three monthly prices in the Stripe sandbox, hosted checkout, the customer portal for cancelling, each Stripe message verified and handled once, four events (first payment, renewal, failed, ended), test mode until the owner goes live (owner, September 28; the mechanics the assistant's design). A failed renewal freezes at the paid-through date, so nobody runs for free and nobody is cut off early (assistant's default).
- Spending guards in code: a cost row before every paid call, a claim per scheduled run, at most two tries then failed, pools stopping at zero in the same write, the freeze in every scheduler and sender, one delivery record per story per person, X and Stripe messages verified and recorded once, a kill switch, so no loop can spend unwatched (owner, September 28). A source costing over $1 in a day is paused, never a merely busy one (owner, September 28, accepting the assistant's rule; a cheap price-alert feed is a later issue).
- The AI Gateway budget is a backstop, not the guard: Vercel lets the crossing request finish, takes minutes to apply, and ignores provider-billed usage, so the code's reservations are what stops spend (Vercel's documentation). $5 a day (assistant's default), set by the owner before live use.
- Four Slack alerts for the test period: X spend over $2 in a day, X balance under $20, no polling run in an hour, any build refused, because with five accounts normal X spend is about $1.15 a day (owner, September 28; ad-period numbers later).
- The owner and Kush start from the blank landing page like strangers; warm DMs go to the three creators; no special seats, so what they test is what customers get (owner, September 28).
- Sign-in with an email that has no agent leads to the landing box; joining two sign-in methods comes later (owner, September 28).
- Contact stores the message and emails no-reply@oparax.ai; the mail password is added by the owner tomorrow and nothing else waits on it (owner, September 28).
- Star thresholds are in the GitHub digest, 3,000 by default, because the owner asked for them on September 16 (owner, September 28).
- Product Hunt's commercial-use email goes out at the first paying user, not before (owner, September 22).
- Analytics: one named event per step of the journey (arrived, built, first view, bot connected, checkout started, paid, cancelled, contact sent) so the funnel can be read in PostHog, the one dashboard (owner).

## Open questions

None.

## Out of scope

Ad campaigns (prepared and launched later at the owner's word; the events above carry the tags they will need), the yearly price, Claude Design restyling, a test suite, clicking a skipped article back, re-reading articles after publishers edit them (a correction reaches a card only through a new report), typed corrections in words, a beat without a handle, joining two sign-in methods, more than one agent per person, teams, email or Slack as alert channels, Bright Data.

## Components, in the order they build

1. Data model: every table, the shared source table loaded from the seed, the cost ledger, the kill switch. Waits on nothing.
2. Core: the spending layer every paid call goes through, the X and Jev clients, the scheduled jobs declared, the packages installed. Waits on the data model.
3. Onboarding: a handle and a sentence build a saved agent and its brief, guarded and priced, with progress that survives a closed tab. Waits on core.
4. Collect: sites and feeds checked every minute for everyone, articles fetched and read whole, day zero. Waits on core.
5. Feed: every item judged, grouped into stories, written into checked cards, both views. Waits on core.
6. Page: the agent page with its building state, both feeds, digests, days left, pool line, the freeze with prices, the story link, the view that starts the clock. Waits on core.
7. Access: checkout, the account by email with a sign-in link, the portal, email, Google and X sign-in. Waits on core.
8. Accounts: watched X accounts polled within the pool, paused at zero. Waits on core.
9. Alerts: the bot, its activation, incoming messages, one DM per story at the tier's cadence. Waits on core.
10. Settings: after paying, the source editor, the account picker, the DM hour, the digest switches. Waits on core.
11. Digests: GitHub and Product Hunt cards and star thresholds. Waits on core.
12. Landing: the landing page, the box, the example agent, Contact, the privacy and terms text. Waits on core.

## The settled scope record (verbatim, .feature/pair-149/scope-settled.md)

# Combined scope candidate for #149 (turn 2: confirm or name one remaining objection)

## Decision record (each disagreement, the resolution, the reason)

1. Cron ownership (A): every worker owns its own cron route; `core` owns `vercel.json` (every path declared up front, with its auth: `Authorization: Bearer CRON_SECRET`), the run-claim helper and the "recover my unfinished records first" contract each worker follows. Reason: no serialization, no shared file.
2. Page seams (B): the page reads rows and posts to route paths; it depends on `data-model` and `core` only. `settings` owns `app/[handle]/settings/**`; `page` owns `app/[handle]/page.tsx`, `app/[handle]/[story]/page.tsx` and `components/monitor/**`; no shared `app/[handle]/layout.tsx` exists (each page composes the existing header and footer). Activation binds only when the sender's immutable X user id equals the monitor's onboarded `x_user_id`.
3. One `feed` component (C): fit, group, adds, the writer and checks. Digests write deterministic cards: the name, a why-now line built from numbers ("+1,200 stars this week", "#2 on Product Hunt today"), and the source's own description or tagline verbatim (at most two lines, cut at a sentence end); judged by Jev fit only; `digests` depends on `data-model` and `core`.
4. Trial watches (D): the accounts onboarding recommended are watched during the free week within the 300-post pool; watched-account polling begins at the first outside view (the clock), never in the dormant days; sites, feeds and the feed engine run from build (owner). Confirmation line to the owner.
5. R4 out (E), with the consequence stated to the owner: a correction published to an already-collected article reaches the card only when a new item reports it. Corrections: "do not filter this source" (owner, September 26) in the source editor; typed corrections in words are a question to the owner, default out of this build.
6. Profile trim (F) and star thresholds (G): questions with defaults (trim; defer), the second stated as deferring his own September 16 request.
7. Gateway budgets (H): stated to the owner as Vercel documents them (soft, delayed, BYOK excluded); the ledger's reservations are the stop.
8. File ownership: `core` creates `lib/x/client.ts`, `lib/ai/jev.ts`, `lib/guards/**`, `lib/monitor-state.ts`, installs every shadcn primitive the plan names (`components/ui/` has one owner), owns `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `vercel.json`, `next.config.ts`, `app/layout.tsx`, `instrumentation-client.ts`, `lib/observability/**`, `lib/x/handle.ts`; `onboarding` alone edits `lib/onboarding/**` and replaces its internal X calls; `landing` owns `lib/landing/content.ts`, `components/landing/**`, `lib/legal/content.ts`, `app/page.tsx`, `app/opengraph-image.tsx`, `lib/contact/**`, `app/api/contact/**`; `access` owns `app/login/**`, `app/signup/**`, `app/forgot-password/**`, `app/auth/**`, `lib/auth/**` (login copy moves to `lib/auth/content.ts` in access's first step; `landingContent.auth` is then dead and landing deletes it in its own step, recorded as a seam), `app/checkout/**`, `app/api/stripe/**`, `lib/billing/**`, `components/posthog-user-context.tsx`. Header and footer signatures (`LandingHeader({signedIn})`, `LandingFooter()`) are frozen seams.
9. Two views (Astra): the page carries both views of the articles feed, single-source cards (one per fitting item) and grouped stories, with a switch (owner, September 22); the feed engine writes a single-source card for every fitting item and the story card for the group. Cost: one writer call per fitting item plus one per story write.
10. Reservation per anonymous build: $3 (assistant's default), the arithmetic from cogs.md unit prices: onboarding $0.278, 300 posts × $0.005 = $1.50, 7 daily DMs × $0.015 = $0.105, judging and writing for up to three weeks under $0.75; rounded up. The owner is asked to let the host add this line to cogs.md section 6 before the plan is written.
11. Watchdog: pauses a source above 200 items a day (a loop signal); a page above 300 watched posts a day raises an alert only, because the monthly pool already bounds it (Astra's point; assistant's numbers).
12. Test seats: owner-built pages for Farzan and Kush carry tier `test` with Creator's pool and cadence, no Stripe, still metered; a question to the owner with that default.
13. Stripe: prices by lookup key are created idempotently by code at first use with the sandbox key (a code path, not a dashboard step); the webhook endpoint and its signing secret are owner-at-ship (or `stripe listen` for the localhost walk).
14. Bot transport (settled with Astra, turn 3): unresolved, and a prerequisite to the detailed plan. The owner is asked to authorize one outbound compatibility check now: one classic DM from `@oparax_ai` to his own X account with `X_BOT_BEARER_TOKEN` (at most about 3 cents: one user lookup and one send at cogs.md unit prices), run by the host and recorded. A successful send establishes outbound compatibility only. Before the detailed plan, separately establish how the existing project bot receives activation and STOP messages through the chosen webhook path, and record token expiry and rotation (a bot token is minted with `expires_at` and a replacement revokes the previous one; docs.x.com/xchat/bots). Any additional external action is named and authorized separately. If either direction remains unsupported, return to the owner with the documented alternatives and their scope, hosting and cost consequences. Do not assume the project bot can complete user OAuth (it has no password or login), or silently introduce a persistent stream.
15. Digest switches: the brief says "a paying or trial person"; the attributed ruling says a signed-up person. Trial pages are read-only (no account), so switches are after checkout; stated to the owner as a confirmation.

## The owner's questions (final list, defaults marked)

1. Watched X posts now: judged by fit and shown as post cards, never DM'd or grouped (default), or shown raw.
2. Sites and feeds polled every 5 minutes (default) or every minute.
3. The daily DM hour: 14:00 UTC for everyone (default), changeable in settings after paying.
4. Contact: stored, a PostHog event, and a PostHog-to-Slack destination configured at ship (default); or also email (needs SMTP credentials).
5. The proof monitor on the landing page: his own handle (default).
6. A page bound to a checkout email: a different-email sign-in sees "this page belongs to the account that paid for it; sign in with that email" (default); provider linking later.
7. Fit check reads the beat and the profile bio (default), or also the 10 onboarding posts (stored at onboarding, reused, not refetched).
8. Trim the profile request and drop the affiliation fetch (default: trim; reverses "pull everything").
9. Farzan and Kush: test seats (default) or paying.
10. AI Gateway budget amounts (the brief's $5 daily now, $10 daily and $100 monthly at launch) for the at-ship command; soft caps per Vercel's docs.
11. Skipped item flip-back: not now (default). Card change on join: the writer's rewrite (R31) already folds and deduplicates (default).
12. R4 six-hourly refetch: out (default), with the consequence above.
13. Typed corrections in words: out (default); "do not filter this source" in.
14. GitHub star thresholds (his September 16 ask): deferred (default) or in.
15. Confirmations: trial watches the recommended accounts within 300; trial pages read-only; digest switches after checkout; the $3 reservation line added to cogs.md; the watchdog pauses sources only.

# Shared contracts for plan <N>

## 1. Table shapes (built by data-model; the only SQL in the plan)

All tables in `public`, RLS enabled on every one, deny-all by default (no policy for `anon`; engines and pages read and write through the admin client, server-only). The only policies: `monitors` SELECT and UPDATE for `authenticated` where `(select auth.uid()) = user_id` (UPDATE with the same WITH CHECK, limited by the settings actions to the columns they own); `monitor_sources`, `monitor_accounts`, `followed_repos` SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE for `authenticated` where the row's `monitor_id` belongs to a monitor with `user_id = (select auth.uid())`. `citext` already exists in the project. Timestamps are `timestamptz`, default `now()` where named `created_at`/`updated_at`.

- `sources`: `id text primary key` (the seed's id, or `x-<handle lowercase>` for an account added later, or `web-<host>-<sha1 of url, 8 chars>` for a site added later), `kind text not null check (kind in ('rss','website','x_account'))`, `target text not null`, `name text not null`, `focus text not null default ''`, `lang text not null default 'en'`, `description text not null default ''`, `items_per_week numeric`, `posts_per_day numeric`, `followers integer`, `recent_titles jsonb not null default '[]'`, `x_user_id text`, `etag text`, `last_modified text`, `last_fetched_at timestamptz`, `next_fetch_at timestamptz not null default now()`, `last_item_at timestamptz`, `unreadable_streak integer not null default 0`, `paused_at timestamptz`, `paused_reason text`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. Index `(next_fetch_at) where paused_at is null`. Seeded from `docs/source-table-seed.json` (150 rows) by the migration (`insert ... on conflict (id) do update` on the descriptive columns only).
- `monitors`: `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`, `handle citext not null unique`, `display_handle text not null`, `x_user_id text`, `beat text not null`, `profile jsonb` (`{name, bio, image, site}`), `posts jsonb` (the 10 onboarding posts as the engine's Post objects, for resume and for nothing else), `brief text`, `status text not null check (status in ('building','failed','live','paused','frozen'))`, `build_step integer not null default 0`, `build_log jsonb not null default '[]'` (array of `{step, message, at}`), `build_error text`, `build_started_at`, `build_finished_at`, `built_by uuid references auth.users(id)`, `user_id uuid references auth.users(id)`, `tier text not null default 'free' check (tier in ('free','hobby','creator','wire'))`, `trial_started_at timestamptz`, `last_viewed_at timestamptz`, `paid_through timestamptz`, `stripe_customer_id text`, `stripe_subscription_id text`, `subscription_status text`, `pool_limit integer not null default 300`, `pool_used integer not null default 0`, `pool_period_start timestamptz`, `cadence text not null default 'daily' check (cadence in ('daily','every_15m'))`, `alert_hour integer not null default 14 check (alert_hour between 0 and 23)`, `alert_timezone text` (IANA name; null means UTC), `last_alert_at timestamptz`, `bot_x_user_id text`, `bot_state text not null default 'none' check (bot_state in ('none','active','paused','stopped'))`, `bot_code text`, `digest_github boolean not null default false`, `digest_product_hunt boolean not null default false`, `github_topics jsonb`, `prefill_done boolean not null default false`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. Indexes: `(user_id)`, `(status)`, `(stripe_customer_id)`.
- `monitor_sources`: `monitor_id uuid references monitors(id) on delete cascade`, `source_id text references sources(id)`, `score numeric`, `why text not null default ''`, `added_by text not null check (added_by in ('onboarding','person'))`, `no_filter boolean not null default false`, `removed_at timestamptz`, `created_at`; primary key `(monitor_id, source_id)`.
- `monitor_accounts`: `monitor_id uuid references monitors(id) on delete cascade`, `handle citext not null`, `x_user_id text`, `name text not null default ''`, `score numeric`, `why text not null default ''`, `watched boolean not null default false`, `posts_per_day numeric`, `counts_checked_at timestamptz`, `since_id text`, `created_at`; primary key `(monitor_id, handle)`.
- `items`: `id text primary key` (articles: `sha1` hex of the canonical final URL per R1; posts: `x:<post id>`), `source_id text not null references sources(id)`, `kind text not null check (kind in ('article','post'))`, `url text not null`, `final_url text`, `title text not null default ''`, `text text not null default ''`, `published_at timestamptz not null`, `date_source text`, `text_from text`, `outcome text not null check (outcome in ('full','short','unreadable'))`, `unreadable_reason text`, `image text`, `lang text`, `author jsonb` (posts: `{handle, name, x_user_id}`), `created_at`. Index `(source_id, published_at desc)`.
- `monitor_items`: `monitor_id uuid`, `item_id text references items(id)`, `status text not null check (status in ('pending','skipped','on','attached','storied','failed'))`, `fit_score numeric`, `fit_band text check (fit_band in ('on','off','unsure'))`, `story_id uuid`, `card jsonb` (the single-source card, verified shape below), `card_status text check (card_status in ('written','no_card','write_failed'))`, `error text`, `tries integer not null default 0`, `judged_at timestamptz`, `created_at`; primary key `(monitor_id, item_id)`; index `(monitor_id, status)`.

Index `(monitor_id, last_published_at desc)`, `(monitor_id, alerted_at) where alerted_at is null`.
- `story_items`: `story_id uuid references stories(id) on delete cascade`, `item_id text references items(id)`, `role text not null check (role in ('opened','joined','attached'))`, `join_score numeric`, `adds_score numeric`, `added_at timestamptz not null default now()`; primary key `(story_id, item_id)`.
- `card_versions`: `id bigint generated always as identity primary key`, `story_id uuid references stories(id) on delete cascade`, `version integer not null`, `card jsonb`, `record jsonb not null` (the write record: prompts, attempts, drops, scores, repair, `headline_from`, `final`), `created_at`; unique `(story_id, version)`.
- `deliveries`: `monitor_id uuid`, `story_id uuid references stories(id) on delete cascade`, `state text not null check (state in ('sending','sent','failed','held'))`, `dm_event_id text`, `attempted_at timestamptz not null default now()`, `sent_at timestamptz`, `tries integer not null default 1`, `error text`; primary key `(monitor_id, story_id)`; index `(sent_at)`.
- `dm_events`: `event_id text primary key`, `monitor_id uuid`, `sender_x_user_id text`, `text text`, `received_at timestamptz not null default now()`, `handled text` (`activated`, `stop`, `pause`, `resume`, `ignored`, `logged`), `payload jsonb`.
- `stripe_events`: `id text primary key`, `type text not null`, `received_at timestamptz not null default now()`, `processed_at timestamptz`, `error text`.
- `cost_ledger`: `id bigint generated always as identity primary key`, `service text not null check (service in ('x','gateway','jev','reservation','stripe'))`, `kind text not null`, `units integer not null default 1`, `usd numeric(10,6) not null`, `monitor_id uuid`, `source_id text`, `run_id text`, `external_id text`, `day date not null default (now() at time zone 'utc')::date`, `hour timestamptz not null default date_trunc('hour', now())`, `created_at`; unique index `(service, external_id, day) where external_id is not null`; index `(day)`, `(monitor_id, day)`, `(source_id, day)`.
- `run_claims`: `job text primary key`, `run_id text not null`, `claimed_at timestamptz not null`, `expires_at timestamptz not null`.
- `config`: `key text primary key`, `value jsonb not null`, `updated_at`. Keys seeded: `kill_switch` `false`; `anon_budget_usd` `200`; `x_balance` `{"console": null, "estimate": null, "checked_at": null}`; `bot` `{"x_user_id": null, "handle": null}`; `stripe_prices` `{}`.
- `contact_messages`: `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`, `email text not null`, `message text not null`, `created_at`, `emailed_at timestamptz`, `error text`.
- `handle_waitlist`: `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`, `handle citext not null`, `beat text`, `created_at`.
- `digest_items`: `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`, `monitor_id uuid references monitors(id) on delete cascade`, `kind text not null check (kind in ('github_repo','product_hunt','star_threshold'))`, `external_id text not null`, `name text not null`, `url text not null`, `why_now text not null`, `description text not null default ''`, `fit_score numeric`, `created_at`; unique `(monitor_id, kind, external_id)`.
- `followed_repos`: `monitor_id uuid references monitors(id) on delete cascade`, `repo text not null` (`owner/name`), `threshold integer not null default 3000`, `stars integer`, `crossed_at timestamptz`, `checked_at timestamptz`, `created_at`; primary key `(monitor_id, repo)`.

RPCs (all `security definer`, `set search_path = public`, `revoke execute from public, anon, authenticated` so only the service role calls them; the admin client calls `.rpc(name, args)`):

- `reserve_build(p_monitor uuid, p_usd numeric) returns boolean`: in one statement, sums `cost_ledger` rows with `service = 'reservation'` and, if that sum plus `p_usd` is at most `config.anon_budget_usd`, inserts the reservation row (`kind = 'build'`, `monitor_id = p_monitor`, `external_id = p_monitor::text`) and returns true; else false. Locks with `select ... for update` on the config row.
- `consume_pool(p_monitor uuid, p_posts integer) returns table (remaining integer, paused boolean)`: `update monitors set pool_used = least(pool_limit, pool_used + p_posts) where id = p_monitor returning pool_limit - pool_used, pool_used >= pool_limit`.
- `claim_run(p_job text, p_run_id text, p_ttl_seconds integer) returns boolean`: inserts or updates `run_claims` only when no unexpired claim exists for `p_job`; returns whether the claim was taken.
- `release_run(p_job text, p_run_id text) returns void`: deletes the claim if it belongs to `p_run_id`.
- `user_id_by_email(p_email text) returns uuid`: `select id from auth.users where lower(email) = lower(p_email) limit 1`.

## 2. The cost ledger (owned by core; every paid caller writes through it)

`recordCost({service, kind, units, usd, monitorId?, sourceId?, runId?, externalId?})` (seam S1) inserts one row; a duplicate `(service, external_id, day)` is ignored (X bills a post or profile once per UTC day). Every paid call writes its row BEFORE or in the same tick as the call that costs (the X client writes after the response with the ids X returned, and writes a `kind = 'request'` row of $0 before the request so an interrupted call still leaves a trace). PostHog receives one `spend_recorded` event per row (properties: service, kind, usd, monitor_id, source_id).

| Paid call | service | kind | unit price (cogs.md) | who pays |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| X user lookup | x | user | $0.010 | the page's reservation (trial) or the tier |
| X post read (timeline, search, tweets) | x | post | $0.005 per returned post id, once per UTC day | same |
| X counts request | x | counts | $0.005 | the tier (picker) |
| X DM send | x | dm_send | $0.015 | same |
| X DM received (webhook event) | x | dm_received | $0.010 | same |
| Gateway model call (Luna fast, Qwen) | gateway | the stage name (`onboarding`, `brief`, `write`, `repair`, `topics`) | read from `providerMetadata.gateway.cost` (fallback `marketCost`) | same |
| Jev request | jev | the question set (`rank`, `fit`, `group`, `adds`, `support`, `headline`, `digest`) | read from the evaluate response's `providerMetadata` | same |
| Anonymous build | reservation | build | $3.00 (assistant's arithmetic in the owner plan) | the $200 total |

Caps and floors (all read at the top of every paid path, seam S2 `guards()`):
- Kill switch: `config.kill_switch = true` stops every paid path and every cron run (they exit with `run_skipped` and a PostHog event `run_skipped` reason `kill_switch`).
- Anonymous builds: `reserve_build` against `config.anon_budget_usd` = 200 (owner, September 28: "$200 total"); reservation $3 per build. A refused reservation is `build_refused` (PostHog) and the box shows the copy in section 5.
- X credit floor: `config.x_balance.estimate < 15` (owner's ruling, the number the assistant's inside it) pauses anonymous builds and trial-tier watched-account polling; paid tiers continue. The estimate is the console balance from the last daily read minus the ledger's X spend since that read.
- Daily credits read: `GET /2/usage/credits` once a day (core's cron) writes `config.x_balance.console = data.total_balance` and `checked_at`; a failed read keeps the last value and the estimate keeps counting down.
- Source watchdog (core's daily cron): a source whose `cost_ledger` rows sum to more than $1.00 on the current UTC day (owner, September 28, accepting the assistant's rule) gets `paused_at = now()`, `paused_reason = 'spend'`; PostHog `source_paused`. No Slack alert of its own (the owner's four only).
- Tries: any model or X call fails after the Gateway's or the client's own single retry → the work item is marked `failed` with the error after 2 tries in total (`tries` column), never a third.

## 3. Clock, pool and cadence rules (owned by core's `monitorState`, seam S3)

`monitorState(m: MonitorRow, now: Date)` returns one of: `building`, `failed`, `dormant` (built, no outside view yet, less than 14 days since `build_finished_at`), `paused` (dormant for 14 days or more; owner, September 28), `trial` (with `daysLeft`, `trialEndsAt = trial_started_at + 7 days`), `frozen` (trial over, `paid_through` null or past), `paid` (`paid_through > now`), `lapsed` (`paid_through` passed after a paid period; treated as frozen with the renewal copy). Rules every scheduler and sender apply:

| Work | dormant | trial | paid | paused, frozen, lapsed, building, failed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| collect (sites and feeds) and feed (judge, group, write) | runs (owner: "the feed runs from build") | runs | runs | no |
| watched X accounts polling | no (nothing spent before a view) | runs, pool 300 | runs, pool by tier | no |
| bot sends | no | runs | runs | no |
| digests | no | no (switches need an account) | runs when switched on | no |

Pool: `pool_limit` 300 in trial; 100 / 3,000 / 4,000 by tier after payment (owner, September 28); `pool_used` reset to 0 and `pool_period_start = now()` on every `invoice.paid`. At zero the accounts pause (no polling) until the reset; sites continue. Cadence: `daily` for free, hobby, creator; `every_15m` for wire; the daily slot is `alert_hour` (default 14) in `alert_timezone` (set from the `x-vercel-ip-timezone` header at the first outside view; null → UTC). The free week's first outside view: `trial_started_at` set once, `last_viewed_at` on every view, by the beacon route (page component) after the bot check and the owner exclusion (`OWNER_EMAILS`). X polling cadence: every minute for wire, every 5 minutes otherwise (owner, September 28; the app limit is 10,000 requests per 15 minutes). Sites and feeds: every minute (owner, September 28). X DM limits (docs.x.com): 1,440 sends per app per day, 15 per recipient per 15 minutes.

## 4. Ids: routes, jobs, events, env

Routes (public unless noted):
- `/` landing; `/<handle>` the agent page; `/<handle>/<storyId>` the page with that story in view; `/<handle>/settings` (signed-in owner of the page only); `/login`, `/signup`, `/forgot-password`, `/auth/confirm`, `/auth/reset-password`; `/checkout/return?session_id=` ; `/privacy`, `/terms`.
- `POST /api/build` (form post: `handle`, `beat`; redirects 303 to `/<handle>`, or to `/?closed=1` when refused, or to `/?handle=&error=` on validation); `POST /api/build/retry` (json `{monitorId}`); `POST /api/view` (json `{monitorId}`, bot-checked); `POST /api/stripe/checkout` (json `{monitorId, tier}` → `{url}`); `POST /api/stripe/webhook` (raw body); `POST /api/stripe/portal` (signed-in owner → `{url}`); `GET|POST /api/x/webhook` (CRC and events); `POST /api/contact` (json `{email, message}`); crons under `/api/cron/*` below.
- Reserved handles (refused by the build route and never routed as pages): `login, signup, forgot-password, auth, checkout, privacy, terms, api, settings, onboarding, opengraph-image, favicon.ico, icon.svg, apple-icon.png, robots.txt, sitemap.xml, _next, static, public, admin, oparax, oparax_ai`; kept in `lib/x/handle.ts` as `RESERVED_HANDLES` (core).

Cron jobs (declared once in `vercel.json` by core; each route checks `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}`, takes `claim_run(job, runId, ttl)` and releases it; every route exports `maxDuration = 300` except where noted):

| path | schedule | owner | job name | ttl |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `/api/cron/collect` | `* * * * *` | collect | `collect` | 240 s |
| `/api/cron/feed` | `* * * * *` | feed | `feed` | 240 s |
| `/api/cron/accounts` | `* * * * *` | accounts | `accounts` | 240 s |
| `/api/cron/alerts` | `* * * * *` | alerts | `alerts` | 120 s |
| `/api/cron/digests` | `0 6 * * *` | digests | `digests` | 600 s (maxDuration 800) |
| `/api/cron/credits` | `0 5 * * *` | core | `credits` | 120 s |

Every cron run writes PostHog `run_completed` (properties: job, run_id, counts) or `run_failed` (job, run_id, error); a skipped claim writes nothing (another run holds it).

PostHog events (one per intent; `snake_case`, past tense; server-side through `lib/analytics/events.ts` seam S4 `track(event, props, distinctId?)`; browser-side only where named): `agent_build_requested` (browser, the box), `agent_build_started`, `agent_built`, `agent_build_failed`, `build_refused` (reason: `budget`, `credits`, `kill_switch`, `bot`, `reserved_handle`, `duplicate`), `trial_started`, `page_viewed_outside` (first view only), `bot_connected`, `bot_paused`, `bot_stopped`, `story_alerted`, `delivery_held`, `paywall_shown` (browser), `checkout_started`, `payment_succeeded`, `payment_failed`, `subscription_cancelled`, `signin_link_sent`, `source_added`, `source_removed`, `source_no_filter_changed`, `account_watch_changed`, `alert_hour_changed`, `digest_switched`, `repo_followed`, `contact_sent`, `spend_recorded`, `source_paused`, `run_completed`, `run_failed`, `run_skipped`. The four Slack alerts at ship (owner): daily sum of `spend_recorded.usd` where service = x over $2; `config.x_balance.console` under $20 (emitted as `x_balance_read` daily with `usd`); no `run_completed` with job `collect` in an hour; any `build_refused`.

Environment (names; values never in git): existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`, `X_BEARER_TOKEN` (app read), `X_BOT_BEARER_TOKEN` (the bot's own token; sends DMs; proven September 28), `X_CLIENT_SECRET` (signs the Activity API CRC and events), `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `PRODUCT_HUNT_TOKEN`. New, owner adds at ship (each slice's part 4 repeats only its own): `CRON_SECRET`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `OWNER_EMAILS` (comma-separated), `SMTP_USER`, `SMTP_PASSWORD`. Code reads `x-vercel-ip-timezone` for the timezone.

## 5. Copy (every shared user-facing string; a component's private strings live in its own content module)

- Handle not found: `Handle not found` (owner, September 27).
- Box closed: `Builds are unavailable right now. Leave your handle and we will tell you when they reopen.` Button `Leave my handle`.
- Building steps (the page's chain of steps): `Looking up @{handle} on X`, `Reading @{handle}'s newest posts`, `Choosing sources and X accounts`, `Writing the brief`.
- Build failed: `Building stopped at: {step}. {reason}` Button `Try again`.
- Trial line: `{n} days left in your free week. Plans from $5 a month.` (n from `monitorState`).
- Frozen banner title: `Your free week is over.` Body: `Your agent has stopped watching. Old cards stay readable. Pick a plan to keep it running.` Buttons: `Hobby, $5 a month`, `Creator, $30 a month`, `Wire, $99 a month`. Tier lines under each: `100 watched X posts a month, one DM a day` / `3,000 watched X posts a month, one DM a day` / `4,000 watched X posts a month, a digest every 15 minutes`. Everywhere: `Sites and feeds unlimited.`
- Lapsed banner: `Payment failed. Update your card to keep your agent running.` Button `Update card`.
- Paused (dormant 14 days): `This agent paused because nobody visited it. It is starting again now.`
- Pool: `{used} of {limit} watched posts used this month.` At zero: `Your watched X accounts are paused until {reset date}. Sites and feeds keep running.`
- Source health: `Could not read its last {n} items.`
- Skipped item: `{score} against your beat`.
- Unverified report: `Unverified report` (an item with no surviving fact), with its link and language tag.
- Further reports on a story: `{n} further reports`.
- Bot button: `Get alerts on X`; helper: `Opens X with a one-time code addressed to @oparax_ai. Send it from @{handle} to connect.` Connected: `Alerts on. Reply STOP to the bot to stop.`
- DM text (one DM, up to 10 stories, assistant's default): first line `Oparax: {n} new {story|stories} for you`, then per story `{headline}\n{first fact}\n{link}` separated by a blank line. Link: `https://oparax.ai/{handle}/{storyId}`.
- Bot replies (as DMs from the bot): to STOP `Alerts stopped. Reply RESUME to start again.`; to PAUSE `Alerts paused. Reply RESUME to start again.`; to RESUME `Alerts on.`; to a valid code `Connected. You will get one DM per story.`; to a code from the wrong account `This code belongs to another X account.`
- Checkout return: `You are set. We emailed a sign-in link to {email}. Your agent is running again.` Pending: `Payment received. We are connecting your account; check {email} in a minute.`
- Wrong-account settings: `This page belongs to the account that paid for it. Sign in with that email.`
- No-agent sign-in: the landing box with the line `You have no agent yet. Type your handle and beat to build one.`
- Contact: title `Contact Us`, fields `Email`, `Message`, button `Send`, done `Thanks, we got your message.` (never claims an email was sent), mail error hidden from the person (stored, `error` column).
- Agent naming: every heading uses `agent` (`Your Oparax agent`, `@{handle}'s agent`).

## 6. Integration seams (owner, callers, exact shape)

- S1 `recordCost(input: CostInput): Promise<void>` in `lib/guards/ledger.ts` (core). Callers: core's X client and Jev client (internal), onboarding (Gateway calls), feed (Gateway calls), digests (Gateway calls), alerts (via the X client), access (none). `CostInput = { service: 'x'|'gateway'|'jev'|'reservation'|'stripe'; kind: string; units?: number; usd: number; monitorId?: string; sourceId?: string; runId?: string; externalId?: string }`.
- S2 `guards(): Promise<{ killSwitch: boolean; anonBuildsOpen: boolean; trialPollingOpen: boolean; balanceEstimate: number | null }>` in `lib/guards/guards.ts` (core). Read at the top of every paid path and every cron. Callers: onboarding (build route), accounts, alerts, feed, collect, digests, page (to show the closed box state on redirect).
- S3 `monitorState(m: Pick<MonitorRow, ...>, now?: Date): { state: 'building'|'failed'|'dormant'|'paused'|'trial'|'frozen'|'paid'|'lapsed'; daysLeft: number|null; trialEndsAt: Date|null; poolOpen: boolean; cadence: 'daily'|'every_15m' }` in `lib/monitor-state.ts` (core). Callers: page, settings, collect, feed, accounts, alerts, digests, access.
- S4 `track(event: EventName, props: Record<string, unknown>, distinctId?: string): void` and the `EventName` union in `lib/analytics/events.ts` (core; server-only, via the PostHog Node client with `waitUntil`). Browser events use `posthog.capture` directly with the same names.
- S5 X client in `lib/x/client.ts` (core): `xGet(path, params, opts?: { token?: 'app'|'bot'; monitorId?; sourceId?; runId? })`, `xPost(path, body, opts)`; one request per second queue, one retry on 429; bills through S1 per returned post and user id (the engine's `xBill` moved here) and per DM send; `xCounts(handle): Promise<number>` (posts per day over 7 days, `-is:reply -is:retweet`, one counts request, $0.005); `xUserByHandle(handle)`; `xTimeline(userId, sinceId?, params)` with `exclude=replies,retweets`; `xSendDm(recipientXUserId, text): Promise<{ ok: true; dmEventId: string } | { ok: false; status: number; error: string; uncertain: boolean }>` (uncertain = timeout or 5xx with no body). Callers: onboarding, accounts, alerts, settings, core's credits cron.
- S6 Jev client in `lib/ai/jev.ts` (core): `jev<T extends Record<string, Question>>(state: object, questions: T, opts: { kind: string; monitorId?; sourceId? }): Promise<Record<keyof T, number>>` over `https://ai-gateway.vercel.sh/v1/evaluate` with `typesafe-ai/jev`, schema-checked probabilities, one retry, bills through S1. Batches questions when the state plus questions would exceed 60 questions per request (R10). Callers: onboarding, feed, accounts (none), digests.
- S7 `gatewayCost(providerMetadata): number` in `lib/ai/cost.ts` (core). Callers: onboarding, feed, digests.
- S8 `claimRun(job, ttlSeconds): Promise<{ runId: string; release: () => Promise<void> } | null>` in `lib/guards/claims.ts` (core), over the RPCs. Callers: every cron route.
- S9 The build route contract (onboarding): `POST /api/build` form fields `handle`, `beat`; responses as in section 4. The landing form (landing) posts here; the no-agent sign-in state (access) renders the same form component from landing (`components/landing/build-box.tsx`, exported `BuildBox({ closed?: boolean; error?: string; handle?: string })`).
- S10 The onboarding output rows (onboarding writes, page and settings and accounts and feed read): `monitors.profile`, `monitors.brief`, `monitors.status = 'live'`, `monitors.build_finished_at`, `monitor_sources` rows (`added_by = 'onboarding'`), `monitor_accounts` rows (`watched = true` for every recommended account: owner, the free week watches the recommended accounts), `sources` rows upserted for recommended accounts that are not seed rows (`id = x-<handle>`, `kind = x_account`, `target = https://x.com/<handle>`).
- S11 Items and verdicts (collect and accounts write `items`; feed reads `items` joined to `monitor_sources`/`monitor_accounts` and writes `monitor_items`, `stories`, `story_items`, `card_versions`): a new `items` row is picked up by the feed cron for every monitor that watches its source (`monitor_sources.removed_at is null` for articles; `monitor_accounts.watched` for posts) by inserting `monitor_items (status = 'pending')` on first sight.
- S12 The verified card shape (feed writes; page, alerts and landing read): `{ headline: string; facts: { text: string; support: number; attribution: number; evidence: { item: string; span: string }[] }[]; publishers: { source_id: string; name: string; url: string }[]; image: string | null; headline_from: 'writer'|'title'|'first fact' }`.
- S13 The view beacon (page owns the component and the route): `POST /api/view` `{ monitorId }` → sets `trial_started_at` once, `last_viewed_at`, `alert_timezone` if null, restarts a paused monitor; excluded when the session user's email is in `OWNER_EMAILS`; bot-checked. Emits `trial_started` and `page_viewed_outside` once.
- S14 Checkout (access owns; page calls): the freeze banner's three buttons POST `/api/stripe/checkout` with `{ monitorId, tier }` and follow `url`. The lapsed banner's `Update card` POSTs `/api/stripe/portal` (signed-in) or, signed out, links to `/login?next=/<handle>`.
- S15 The activation link (alerts owns the code and the bot identity; page renders the button): `monitors.bot_code` is set by onboarding? No: by alerts' `ensureBotCode(monitorId)` called from the page at render when null (a 10-character random code, letters and digits); the button links to `https://x.com/messages/compose?recipient_id={config.bot.x_user_id}&text={code}`. `config.bot` is filled by core's credits cron (and on first need) from `GET /2/users/me` with the bot token.
- S16 The single-source card: feed writes `monitor_items.card` in the S12 shape (publishers has one entry) for every item with `fit_band = 'on'`; the story card is separate; the page shows both views.
- S17 Digest rows (`digest_items`, `followed_repos`): digests writes, page reads, settings writes `followed_repos` and the two switches.
- S18 `scripts/register-x-webhook.mjs` (alerts writes into core's folder): registers `https://oparax.ai/api/x/webhook` with the app bearer and subscribes the bot account with the bot token; run by the owner at ship; prints the webhook id and never a secret.
- S19 Header and footer (core owns `components/site-header.tsx` and `components/site-footer.tsx`, moved from `components/landing/landing-header.tsx` and `landing-footer.tsx` with the same props: `SiteHeader({ signedIn })`, `SiteFooter()`); the footer's Contact trigger imports `ContactDialog` from `components/landing/contact-dialog.tsx` (landing owns; its props stay `{ triggerClassName, label? }`). Callers: page, settings, access, landing.
- S20 Login copy: `lib/auth/content.ts` (access) holds every auth string; `landingContent.auth` is deleted by landing in its own step once access has landed (integration checks it).
- S21 `StoryCard({ card, story, handle, compact?: boolean })` in `components/monitor/story-card.tsx` (page); landing imports it for the example agent's three latest stories.
- S22 Reserved handles and validation: `normalizeValidHandle`, `RESERVED_HANDLES`, `isReservedHandle(handle)` in `lib/x/handle.ts` (core). Callers: onboarding, page, landing.
- S23 Mail: `sendMail({ to, subject, text }): Promise<{ ok: boolean; error?: string }>` in `lib/contact/mail.ts` (landing) over `nodemailer` with `smtp.gmail.com:465` and `SMTP_USER`/`SMTP_PASSWORD`; only Contact calls it.
- S24 Stripe prices: `priceIdFor(tier): Promise<string>` in `lib/billing/prices.ts` (access) creates or finds by `lookup_key` (`oparax_hobby_monthly`, `oparax_creator_monthly`, `oparax_wire_monthly`; amounts 500, 3000, 9900 cents, `recurring.interval = month`) and caches ids in `config.stripe_prices`.
- S25 The Activity API event shapes (alerts): CRC `GET ?crc_token=` → `{ response_token: 'sha256=' + base64(hmac_sha256(X_CLIENT_SECRET, crc_token)) }`; events `POST` with header `x-twitter-webhooks-signature-oauth2` = `sha256=` + base64 HMAC of the raw body with `X_CLIENT_SECRET`; the DM event's exact JSON shape is a named build-time check in the alerts slice.

# data-model.md
Skills: supabase, supabase-postgres-best-practices

## 1. Files and contracts

Files: `supabase/migrations/<utc timestamp>_product_tables.sql` (one migration, mirrored after the MCP applies it), `supabase/migrations/<utc timestamp>_seed_sources.sql` (the 150 seed rows as data, generated from `docs/source-table-seed.json`), `lib/supabase/database.types.ts` (regenerated from the live catalog after both). `migrations: yes`.

Contracts: every table, column, index, constraint, policy and RPC exactly as `shared.md` section 1 lists them; nothing else in the schema. RLS enabled on every table with the policies named there and no others. The RPCs are `security definer`, `set search_path = public`, execute revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`. `citext` is already installed (setup.md); `gen_random_uuid()` is available. The seed maps the JSON fields `id, kind, target, name, focus, lang, description, itemsPerWeek → items_per_week, postsPerDay → posts_per_day, followers, recentTitles → recent_titles`; `foundFor` and `groups` are dropped (nothing reads them). `x_user_id` is left null (accounts fill it at first poll).

Input classes: none (no entry point).

Seams cited: section 1 whole; S1 (the ledger's unique index), S3 (the status columns), the RPCs used by S2, S8, access.

## 2. Build steps

1. `$supabase:supabase-postgres-best-practices` then `$supabase:supabase`: write the product-tables migration as SQL intent from `shared.md` section 1: tables in dependency order (`sources`, `monitors`, `monitor_sources`, `monitor_accounts`, `items`, `monitor_items`, `stories`, `story_items`, `card_versions`, `deliveries`, `dm_events`, `stripe_events`, `cost_ledger`, `run_claims`, `config`, `contact_messages`, `handle_waitlist`, `digest_items`, `followed_repos`), then indexes, then `alter table ... enable row level security` on every table, then the four owner policies (`monitors` select/update for `authenticated` on `user_id = (select auth.uid())`, and the three child tables' policies through `exists (select 1 from monitors m where m.id = monitor_id and m.user_id = (select auth.uid()))` for select, insert, update, delete, each with `with check` where the command needs it), then `updated_at` triggers on `sources`, `monitors`, `config`, then the five RPCs (`reserve_build`, `consume_pool`, `claim_run`, `release_run`, `user_id_by_email`) with the revokes, then the `config` seed rows. Apply it with the Supabase MCP `apply_migration` (name `product_tables`), mirror the SQL to the file with the `-- Applied via the Supabase MCP server` header.
2. `$supabase:supabase`: generate the seed migration from `docs/source-table-seed.json` (a small Node one-liner run once, its output committed; no script file kept): 150 `insert into sources ... on conflict (id) do update set name = excluded.name, focus = excluded.focus, lang = excluded.lang, description = excluded.description, items_per_week = excluded.items_per_week, posts_per_day = excluded.posts_per_day, followers = excluded.followers, recent_titles = excluded.recent_titles`. Apply with `apply_migration` (name `seed_sources`), mirror to the file.
3. `$supabase:supabase`: regenerate `lib/supabase/database.types.ts` with the MCP `generate_typescript_types`, replace the file whole (it is generated), and run the MCP `get_advisors` for security; a finding on these tables is fixed in a follow-up migration `<ts>_advisor_fixes.sql` applied and mirrored the same way (an advisor finding about pre-existing auth objects is logged and left).

## 3. Acceptance journeys

- J1: In the Supabase dashboard's table editor the 19 tables exist, `sources` holds 150 rows, every table shows RLS enabled, and `select reserve_build('00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000'::uuid, 3)` in the SQL editor (run as the service role) returns `true` once the config row `anon_budget_usd` is 200, and the ledger gains one `reservation` row.

## 4. Owner does at ship

none

# core.md
Skills: nextjs, vercel-functions, supabase, instrument-integration, instrument-product-analytics, instrument-error-tracking, instrument-llm-analytics, ai-gateway, shadcn

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `lib/guards/ledger.ts` (S1), `lib/guards/guards.ts` (S2), `lib/guards/claims.ts` (S8), `lib/guards/watchdog.ts`, `lib/monitor-state.ts` (S3), `lib/analytics/events.ts` (S4), `lib/x/client.ts` (S5), `lib/x/handle.ts` (S22), `lib/ai/jev.ts` (S6), `lib/ai/cost.ts` (S7), `lib/observability/` (existing files; the reference-init diff), `app/api/cron/credits/route.ts`, `vercel.json` (crons), `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `next.config.ts`, `app/layout.tsx`, `instrumentation-client.ts`, `components/ui/` (primitives added by the CLI only), `components/site-header.tsx`, `components/site-footer.tsx` (S19; moved from `components/landing/landing-header.tsx` and `landing-footer.tsx`, which this component deletes; the landing component's page imports the new names), `scripts/` (the folder, with `scripts/README.md` one paragraph).

Contracts: S1 to S8, S19, S22 exactly as `shared.md` states. `guards()` reads `config` once per call. `recordCost` never throws to its caller (a ledger failure is reported with `reportServerException` and the paid call still proceeds, because the row is a record, not a permission; the permission checks are S2 and the RPCs). The X client's `xSendDm` uses `X_BOT_BEARER_TOKEN` and `POST /2/dm_conversations/with/{recipient}/messages` with `{ text }` (proven September 28) and reads the DM event id from `data.dm_event_id`; every other call uses `X_BEARER_TOKEN`; the one-request-per-second queue from `engine.ts` (`xTurn`) moves here unchanged. `xCounts` uses `GET /2/tweets/counts/recent?query=from:{handle} -is:reply -is:retweet&granularity=day` and returns the 7-day mean. The credits cron: `GET /2/usage/credits` with the app token → `config.x_balance.console = data.total_balance`, `checked_at`; then `config.bot` from `GET /2/users/me` with the bot token when null; then the watchdog (section 2 of shared.md); then the trial pause pass (`monitors` dormant for 14 days → `status = 'paused'`); emits `x_balance_read`.

Input classes: the credits route admits only `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` (401 otherwise). `recordCost` validates its input with a zod schema (Check at the door).

Packages added (one step, `pnpm add`): `stripe`, `botid`, `nodemailer` and `@types/nodemailer`. shadcn primitives added (one step, `pnpm dlx shadcn add`): `card`, `tabs`, `switch`, `table`, `alert`, `skeleton`, `avatar`, `radio-group`, `alert-dialog`, `progress`, `checkbox`, `dropdown-menu`, `sheet`, `scroll-area` (existing: badge, button-group, button, collapsible, dialog, input, label, select, separator, sonner, spinner, textarea, tooltip). No primitive is hand-edited.

## 2. Build steps

1. `$vercel:nextjs`: add the packages with `pnpm add stripe botid nodemailer` and `pnpm add -D @types/nodemailer`; add the primitives with one `pnpm dlx shadcn add card tabs switch table alert skeleton avatar radio-group alert-dialog progress checkbox dropdown-menu sheet scroll-area`. Create `scripts/README.md` (one paragraph: owner-run Node scripts, run with `node scripts/<name>.mjs`, they read `.env.local` and print no secret).
2. `$supabase:supabase`: `lib/guards/ledger.ts` (S1) with the zod `CostInput` schema and the `spend_recorded` event; `lib/guards/claims.ts` (S8) over `claim_run`/`release_run` with `run_id = crypto.randomUUID()`; `lib/guards/guards.ts` (S2) reading `config.kill_switch`, `config.anon_budget_usd` versus the reservation sum, `config.x_balance` → `balanceEstimate = console - (x spend in cost_ledger since checked_at)`, `anonBuildsOpen = !killSwitch && estimate >= 15 (or estimate null) && reservations + 3 <= budget`, `trialPollingOpen = !killSwitch && (estimate == null || estimate >= 15)`.
3. `lib/monitor-state.ts` (S3) as a pure function with the table of shared.md section 3, plus `nextDailySlot(m, now)` (the next `alert_hour` in `alert_timezone` using `Intl.DateTimeFormat` parts, no library) used by alerts.
4. `$posthog:instrument-product-analytics`: `lib/analytics/events.ts` (S4): the `EventName` union from shared.md section 4, `track()` over `getPostHogServerClient()` with `distinctId` defaulting to `server`, `waitUntil`-safe, never throwing.
5. `$vercel:ai-gateway` then `$posthog:instrument-llm-analytics`: `lib/ai/cost.ts` (S7, the `costOf` from `engine.ts`) and `lib/ai/jev.ts` (S6): the `jev()` from `engine.ts` generalized to any question set, zod-checked, batching at 60 questions, one retry, billing through S1 with `service = 'jev'`, and one `captureAiGeneration` per request (model `typesafe-ai/jev`, stage = `opts.kind`).
6. `lib/x/client.ts` (S5): `xTurn`, `xGet`, `xPost`, `xBill` moved from `engine.ts` (the engine keeps its `toPosts`, fields and rendering), `xUserByHandle`, `xTimeline`, `xCounts`, `xSendDm` with the `uncertain` flag on timeout or 5xx without a body; each bills through S1. `lib/x/handle.ts` gains `RESERVED_HANDLES` and `isReservedHandle` (S22).
7. `$vercel:vercel-functions`: `app/api/cron/credits/route.ts` (the contract above; `maxDuration = 300`; the claim `credits`), `lib/guards/watchdog.ts` (sum `cost_ledger.usd` by `source_id` for the current UTC day; pause over $1.00; `source_paused` event); `vercel.json` gains the `crons` array of shared.md section 4 exactly (six entries).
8. `$vercel:nextjs`: `app/layout.tsx` adds the `botid` client component (`BotIdClient` from `botid/client` with `protect` listing `POST /api/build` and `POST /api/view`; the exact export names are a named build-time check against `node_modules/botid/*.d.ts`: if the client component is named differently, use the documented name; if `protect` takes a different shape, follow the `.d.ts`), and `next.config.ts` wraps with `withBotId` from `botid/next/config` when that export exists (same check; if it does not, log the decision). `components/site-header.tsx` and `components/site-footer.tsx` are the moved header and footer (S19), importing `landingContent.brand` still from `lib/landing/content.ts` (read only), and the header's sign-in buttons keep linking to `/login` and `/signup`.
9. Reference-init diff, PostHog: read `posthog:instrument-integration`'s Next.js reference (`references/next-js.md`, the `instrumentation-client.ts` block) and our `lib/observability/posthog-client.ts`; list every option the reference sets that ours does not; add each, or record in the decision log why not (our `before_send` scrubbing, `capture_pageview: 'history_change'` and the replay masking are owner decisions and stay). `$posthog:instrument-error-tracking`: confirm `capture_exceptions: true` remains and that `reportServerException` is exported for the crons; no other change.

## 3. Acceptance journeys

- J2: On localhost, `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET" localhost:3000/api/cron/credits` returns `{ok: true}` and the `config` row `x_balance.console` shows the balance the X console shows (about $119 on September 28, minus the day's spend); a second call within a minute returns `{skipped: 'claimed'}`; the same call without the header returns 401.
- J3: In the Supabase table editor, set `config.kill_switch` to `true`; every cron call now returns `{skipped: 'kill_switch'}`; set it back.

- Add `OWNER_EMAILS` = `farzanmrz@gmail.com,farzan@oparax.ai` to Vercel and `.env.local`.
- Run `vercel ai-gateway budgets set api-key oparax-experiment-1 --limit 5 --refresh-period daily` (the $5 daily is the assistant's default; a soft cap per Vercel's docs).

# onboarding.md
Skills: nextjs, vercel-functions, ai-sdk, ai-gateway, typesafe-ai, prompt-format, supabase

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `lib/onboarding/engine.ts`, `lib/onboarding/prompts.ts`, `lib/onboarding/types.ts`, `lib/onboarding/run.ts` (new: the durable runner), `app/api/build/route.ts`, `app/api/build/retry/route.ts`; deleted: `app/onboarding/page.tsx`, `app/onboarding/onboarding-client.tsx`, `app/api/onboarding/route.ts` (the bare page and its stream; the landing box replaces them; owner, September 28: he builds from the blank page like anyone).

Contracts:
- `POST /api/build` (S9): form fields `handle` (validated by `normalizeValidHandle`, refused when `isReservedHandle`), `beat` (1 to 300 characters, trimmed). Order: kill switch and `guards()` → `build_refused` redirect `303 /?closed=1` when `anonBuildsOpen` is false; bot check (`checkBotId()` from `botid/server`; a bot gets 403); existing `monitors` row for the handle → `303 /<handle>` (one build per handle, owner); else `reserve_build(newMonitorId, 3)` → false: `303 /?closed=1`; true: insert `monitors` (`status = 'building'`, `built_by = session user id or null`, `build_started_at`), `track('agent_build_started')`, `after(runBuild(monitorId))`, `303 /<handle>`. `maxDuration = 800`. A signed-in owner's build is not anonymous: when the session user's email is in `OWNER_EMAILS` the reservation is skipped (his builds for the three creators do not spend the $200) but still ledgered as real cost.
- `POST /api/build/retry` json `{ monitorId }`: only when `status = 'failed'`; restarts `runBuild` from the saved step (no new reservation; the existing one stays; owner plan: only the extra work is charged); `303 /<handle>`.
- `runBuild(monitorId)` in `run.ts`: reads the row, runs the engine's steps with `report(step, message)` appending to `build_log` and setting `build_step`; step 1 saves `profile` and `x_user_id` (and `posts` after step 2) so a retry resumes at the first unsaved step; step 3 writes S10 rows, `brief`, `status = 'live'`, `build_finished_at`, `track('agent_built', { cost_usd })`; any error: `status = 'failed'`, `build_error = <step>: <message>`, `track('agent_build_failed')`, `reportServerException`. Handle not found: `status = 'failed'`, `build_error = 'Handle not found'` and the reservation is released (a `reservation` row with negative usd and `kind = 'release'`).
- The engine: `runOnboarding` keeps its three steps, its prompt and its Jev question; changes: (a) the X calls go through `lib/x/client.ts` (S5) and the in-engine `xTurn`, `xGet`, `xBill`, `costOf` and the `jev()` function are deleted in favor of S5, S6, S7; (b) `WALKTHROUGH_STEPS` and the `writer` parameter are removed; the engine takes `{ handle, beat, report, resume?: { profile?, posts? } }` and returns `{ profile, posts, final, brief, costUsd, turns }`; (c) the profile request is trimmed to `description,entities,id,name,pinned_tweet_id,profile_image_url,url,username` and the `affiliation.user_id` expansion is dropped (owner, September 28); (d) the model's structured answer gains `brief: string` (a detailed brief on the person: who they are, what they follow, in which languages, what they publish, at most 1,200 characters; the assistant's cap) written in the same call as the picks, and `prompts.ts` gains one `<brief>` section describing it (prompt-format rules: sections, placeholders for numbers, no digits typed by hand); (e) the Jev question and lines stay as they are (`POSSIBLE = 0.35`, owner).
- Input classes: the form (validated, rejected with `303 /?error=handle` or `/?error=beat`); the retry json (zod, 400 otherwise); the engine's X responses (already schema-checked through `toPosts`, kept).

Seams cited: S1, S2, S5, S6, S7, S9, S10, S22.

## 2. Build steps

1. `$vercel:ai-sdk` then `$typesafe-ai` then `$prompt-format`: in `engine.ts` remove `WALKTHROUGH_STEPS`, the writer, the in-engine X queue, `xGet`, `xBill`, `costOf` and `jev`; import S5, S6, S7; trim `USER_FIELDS` and the affiliation expansion; add `brief` to the `Answer` schema and the `<brief>` section to `prompts.ts` with `{BRIEF_CHARS}` filled from a constant (1200); return the result object; `report()` calls where `writer.write(data-status)` were.
2. `$supabase:supabase`: `lib/onboarding/run.ts`: `runBuild` as above, writing S10 rows with the admin client (`monitor_sources` from `final.sites`, `monitor_accounts` from `final.accounts` with `watched = true`, `sources` upserts for non-seed accounts), the resume logic from `monitors.profile`/`posts`, the ledger rows for the Gateway calls (`service = 'gateway'`, `kind = 'onboarding'`, usd from S7) and the PostHog AI generation capture (`captureAiGeneration`, stage `onboarding`).
3. `$vercel:vercel-functions` then `$vercel:nextjs`: `app/api/build/route.ts` and `app/api/build/retry/route.ts` as the contracts above (`after()` from `next/server`; `maxDuration = 800`; the bot check's import is a named build-time check against `node_modules/botid/*.d.ts`, same as core step 8).
4. Delete `app/onboarding/` and `app/api/onboarding/`; delete `PageProfile`, `PageMedia`, `OnboardingData`, `OnboardingUIMessage` from `types.ts` (dead once the stream is gone; the page reads `monitors.profile` instead); keep `Final`, `HANDLE_NOT_FOUND`.

## 3. Acceptance journeys

- J4: On localhost, signed out, submit `ottleyai` and `AI developments and practical tools` in the landing box; the browser lands on `/ottleyai` building; within about a minute the page shows the profile, the brief, 9 or so sites and at least 5 accounts (the run cost about $0.28 on September 27); the ledger holds a $3 reservation row, one `x` row per post and profile, one `gateway` row, one `jev` row.
- J5: Submit `ottleyai` again: the browser lands on the existing page and no second `monitors` row exists.
- J6: Submit a handle that does not exist on X (`zzqqxx_none_9`): the page shows `Building stopped at: Looking up @zzqqxx_none_9 on X. Handle not found`, and the ledger shows the reservation and its release.

# collect.md
Skills: nextjs, vercel-functions, supabase

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `lib/sources/discovery.ts`, `lib/sources/feed.ts`, `lib/sources/sitemap.ts`, `lib/sources/article-text.ts`, `lib/http-fetch.ts`, `lib/xml.ts` (existing, edited), `lib/collect/poll.ts`, `lib/collect/listing.ts`, `lib/collect/date.ts`, `lib/collect/item.ts`, `app/api/cron/collect/route.ts`.

Contracts:
- The cron (`collect`, every minute, S8 claim, `guards()` kill switch): selects sources of kind `rss` or `website` with `paused_at is null and next_fetch_at <= now()` that at least one live monitor watches (join `monitor_sources ... removed_at is null` and `monitors.status in ('live')` with `monitorState` in dormant, trial or paid), up to 60 per run (assistant's number), fetched in parallel (8 at once, the lab's number). Each source: a conditional request (`If-None-Match` from `etag`, `If-Modified-Since` from `last_modified`) through `fetchSafeSourceWithFinalUrl` with the user agent changed to a normal Chrome browser string (owner, September 19; `discovery.ts` today sends `Oparax source discovery`); 304 → only `last_fetched_at`, `next_fetch_at = now() + 1 minute`; 200 → entries per R26 (feeds: `pubDate`/`date`/`published` before `updated`; RDF handled; the entry's image from enclosure, media thumbnail or first body image) or R27 (listing links: same site, not navigation paths, article-shaped last segment of at least 8 characters with a hyphen or digit, no file extensions; `discovery.ts`'s query-parameter stripping is limited to the R1 tracking parameters). New candidates (not in `items` by R1 id) have their article page fetched (8 at once per source), dated per R28, titled per R29, read per R30 (the reader in `article-text.ts` is brought to R30: the article or main element first, the 400-character paragraph rule, the 60 percent body rule, quoted passages marked `[quote] ... [end quote]`, denials kept, the title as the first line), given an outcome per R3 (`full` at 400+ characters, `short` at 120 to 399, else `unreadable` with reason `under_120`, `teaser`, `unavailable`, `furniture`), and inserted into `items` (`kind = 'article'`). Undated pages are left out (R26, R28). Source health: `unreadable_streak` counts consecutive unreadable items; a readable one resets it. `next_fetch_at = now() + 1 minute` (owner, September 28). A fetch error: `reportServerException`, `next_fetch_at = now() + 5 minutes`, no state change otherwise.
- Day zero (R25, owner): when a live monitor has `prefill_done = false`, the run fetches each of its sources regardless of `next_fetch_at`, keeps only entries published within the last 2 days, at most the 10 newest per source, inserts them, then sets `prefill_done = true`. Sources of kind `x_account` are never fetched here (accounts owns them).
- Every new `items` row triggers, in the same run, `monitor_items (status = 'pending')` for every monitor watching that source (S11).
- Input classes: the cron header (401 otherwise); every fetched body passes the existing size guards (5 MB) and the SSRF checks; XML and HTML are parsed defensively (a parse failure is `unreadable: unavailable`).

Seams cited: S1 (no paid calls here; fetching is free), S2, S3, S8, S11.

## 2. Build steps

1. `$vercel:nextjs`: `lib/sources/discovery.ts`: the user agent becomes a current Chrome desktop string (owner, September 19); `fetchSafeSourceWithFinalUrl` gains optional `headers` (for the conditional request) and returns `etag`/`last-modified` from the response; the query-parameter stripping follows R1's tracking list only.
2. `lib/sources/feed.ts`: R26 date precedence, RDF (`rdf:RDF` with `item` children) parsed, entry image per shared.md's `items.image` rule; `lib/sources/article-text.ts`: the R30 reader (quotes marked, denials kept, the 400/60 percent/200 rules, title first) with `via` kept for records.
3. `lib/collect/date.ts` (R28), `lib/collect/item.ts` (R1 id, R29 title, R3 outcome), `lib/collect/listing.ts` (R27), `lib/collect/poll.ts` (the per-source flow above, parallel with a concurrency limit of 8 written inline with `Promise.all` over chunks, no library).
4. `$vercel:vercel-functions`: `app/api/cron/collect/route.ts`: `maxDuration = 300`, the claim, the guard, the source selection with day-zero first, `run_completed` with counts (`sources`, `items`, `unreadable`).

## 3. Acceptance journeys

- J7: With the `ottleyai` agent built (J4) and `CRON_SECRET` in `.env.local`, call `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET" localhost:3000/api/cron/collect`; the `items` table gains up to 10 items per source from the last 2 days, each with full text (over 400 characters for most), and `prefill_done` is true; `monitor_items` holds one pending row per item.
- J8: Call it again a minute later: sources answer 304 or return nothing new; `run_completed` shows `items: 0`; a feed source with a new article (check TechCrunch's feed) shows the new item within the next call.

## 4. Owner does at ship

none
