# feed.md
Skills: ai-sdk, ai-gateway, typesafe-ai, prompt-format, instrument-llm-analytics, supabase

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `lib/feed/fit.ts`, `lib/feed/group.ts`, `lib/feed/adds.ts`, `lib/feed/writer.ts`, `lib/feed/prompts.ts`, `lib/feed/checks.ts`, `lib/feed/repair.ts`, `lib/feed/process.ts`, `lib/feed/types.ts`, `app/api/cron/feed/route.ts`.

Contracts (the specification is `docs/downstream-algorithm.md`, its R-rules cited by number; every number here is a code constant, filled into prompts through placeholders per `prompt-format`):
- The cron (`feed`, every minute, S8 claim, `guards()` kill switch): selects monitors in dormant, trial or paid state (S3) with pending `monitor_items`; processes monitors in parallel (8 at once), items within one monitor one at a time in `published_at` order (R13); at most 40 items per monitor per run (assistant's number) so a run stays inside `maxDuration = 300`; the rest wait for the next minute.
- Per item: `items.outcome = 'unreadable'` → `monitor_items.status = 'skipped'`, `fit_band = null`, no call. Else fit (R6 question verbatim, one Jev request per item through S6, `kind = 'fit'`) over the state `{ beat, person: { brief }, source: { name, focus, description, why }, preferences: [], examples: [], item: { title, text, published_at } }` (owner, September 28: the brief and the source's why go to Jev; preferences and examples stay empty this build); lines R7/R8 (`FIT_ON = 0.5`, `FIT_OFF = 0.35`, unsure counts off); a source with `monitor_sources.no_filter = true` is `on` without a call. Off or unsure → `status = 'skipped'` with the score. On → `status = 'on'`, then the single-source card (S16) and grouping.
- Single-source card (owner, September 22: both views): one writer call per fitting item (R15 to R20 with the item alone), checks (R17 code, R18 support and attribution, R19 headline, at `SUPPORT_LINE = 0.5`), one repair (R31), the headline that ships (R32); the verified card in S12 shape written to `monitor_items.card`, `card_status`.
- Grouping (articles and posts alike; owner, September 28): open stories = this monitor's stories with `last_published_at` within `WINDOW_H = 72` hours either way of the item (R10), batched at 60 per Jev request; question R11 verbatim per story (`kind = 'group'`); the item joins every story at `JOIN_LINE = 0.75` or above (assistant's default, recorded); none → a new story (`status = 'open'`, `fallback_title = item title`, `opened_at`, `last_published_at`, `story_items role = 'opened'`) and the story card is written (the same writer path with `<previous_card>` absent). Joined → the adds question R14 (`kind = 'adds'`, `ADDS_LINE = 0.75`): at or above → rewrite the story card (R15 with `<previous_card>`), below → `story_items role = 'attached'`, nothing rewritten. A grouping call failure → `status = 'pending'` with `error = 'grouping failed'` and `tries + 1`; adds failure → same; two tries → `status = 'failed'`.
- Writer: model `alibaba/qwen3.7-flash`, `reasoning: 'medium'` (the lab's accepted option; the exact AI SDK option name is confirmed against `node_modules/ai/docs` at build, a named check), temperature 0, `maxOutputTokens = 6000`, a 240-second abort; the system prompt of section 7 verbatim with `{FACTS_MIN}=1`, `{FACTS_MAX}=5`, `{EVIDENCE_MIN}=1`, `{EVIDENCE_MAX}=3` filled from constants; the user message per R15 with `<beat>`, `<person>` (the brief; owner, September 28), `<previous_card>`, `<item>`; posts render as `<item publisher="@handle (name)">`. Output validated by zod per R20 with one retry carrying `<retry>`. Every call ledgered (S1, `service = 'gateway'`, `kind = 'write'|'repair'`, usd from S7) and captured with `captureAiGeneration` (stage `write`/`repair`, `distinctId = monitor id`).
- Story card save: `stories.version` check (`update ... where version = :v`), `card_versions` row with the full record (R9 section 9 fields), `last_changed_at` on a new or changed fact (R22), `stories.status` in `written|no_card|write_failed`, `image` from the first item with one.
- Watched posts as items: `items.kind = 'post'` rows from accounts go through the same path; `source` for Jev is the account row (`name`, `focus = ''`, `description = bio if known`), and `why` from `monitor_accounts.why`.
- Input classes: the cron header; model output through zod (R20); Jev output through S6's schema.

Seams cited: S1, S2, S3, S6, S7, S8, S11, S12, S16.

## 2. Build steps

# page.md
Skills: nextjs, react-best-practices, shadcn, frontend-design, web-design-guidelines, accessibility, beautiful-shadows, ai-elements, emil-design-eng, instrument-product-analytics

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `app/[handle]/page.tsx`, `app/[handle]/[story]/page.tsx`, `app/[handle]/not-found.tsx`, `app/api/view/route.ts`, `components/monitor/agent-header.tsx`, `components/monitor/building.tsx`, `components/monitor/sources-list.tsx`, `components/monitor/accounts-strip.tsx`, `components/monitor/feed.tsx`, `components/monitor/story-card.tsx` (S21), `components/monitor/item-card.tsx`, `components/monitor/skipped-list.tsx`, `components/monitor/digest-block.tsx`, `components/monitor/state-banner.tsx`, `components/monitor/pay-buttons.tsx`, `components/monitor/bot-button.tsx`, `components/monitor/view-beacon.tsx`, `components/monitor/refresh-while-building.tsx`, `lib/monitor/content.ts` (every string of this surface not in shared.md section 5), `lib/monitor/read.ts` (the server reads).

Screens, one line each (stock shadcn Mira per DESIGN.md; the ui skills' planning decisions follow):
- The building state: the agent header with the handle, then an `ai-elements` `ChainOfThought` of the four steps from `build_log` (`ChainOfThoughtStep` with `status` active/complete, the profile step showing the picture and bio as the old page did), refreshed by `refresh-while-building.tsx` (`router.refresh()` every 3 seconds while `status = 'building'`, no websocket); motion: only the step shimmer (`emil-design-eng`: motion answers a live step). Failed: an `Alert` (destructive) with the copy and a `Try again` button posting to `/api/build/retry`.
- The page: header (`SiteHeader`, S19), `agent-header` (avatar `Avatar` 48 px, name, `@handle` in mono, the beat, the brief in a `Card`), `state-banner` (trial line, frozen banner with `pay-buttons` as three `Card`s each with a `Button`, lapsed banner, paused line, pool line; semantic colors: amber for days left under 3, red for frozen), `bot-button` (a `Button` linking to X, or the connected line), `Tabs` with `Stories` and `Articles` (the two views; the tab row is a real `Tabs`, keyboard reachable), the feed (`StoryCard` and `ItemCard` as `Card`s with `beautiful-shadows` medium, headline in Nunito Sans, facts as a list with the publisher name and a link per fact's source, `{n} further reports` as a `Collapsible`, the time in mono, an image at the top when present, posts with the account's handle and name), the skipped list (a `Collapsible` "Skipped" with score badges), `sources-list` (each source: name, focus, the why sentence, health line), `accounts-strip` (handles as `Badge`s, `watched` ones marked), `digest-block` (when switches are on), `SiteFooter`. Touch targets 44 px under 700 px (`accessibility`); AA contrast from the tokens; every icon-only control labelled; `desk:` for the two-column layout above 700 px (feed left, sources and accounts right, `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_360px]`).
- The story link `/[handle]/[story]`: the same page with that story's card first and marked (`aria-current`), the tab set to Stories, and `scroll-margin-top` for the sticky header; 404 for an unknown story.
- Not found: `/[handle]` with no agent → `not-found.tsx` with the line `No agent for @{handle} yet.` and a link to `/`.

Contracts:
- Reads (`lib/monitor/read.ts`, admin client, server components only; nothing per-user leaves the server): the monitor row minus `stripe_*`, `bot_code`, `posts`, `build_log` (the building state reads `build_log` separately); `monitor_sources` joined to `sources` (health from `unreadable_streak`); `monitor_accounts`; the newest 60 stories with cards (`status = 'written'` or `no_card` with `fallback_title`), ordered by `last_changed_at desc`; the newest 60 `monitor_items` with cards for the Articles view, ordered by `published_at desc`; skipped items with scores (newest 30); `digest_items` (newest 30); the caller's session (for the owner exclusion and the settings link when `user_id` matches).
- The view beacon (S13): `view-beacon.tsx` POSTs `/api/view` once after mount (not on the owner's session: the server passes `isOwner` so the beacon is not rendered for him); the route checks `checkBotId()`, the session email against `OWNER_EMAILS`, then updates `trial_started_at` (once, when `status = 'live'` and null), `last_viewed_at`, `alert_timezone` from `x-vercel-ip-timezone` when null, and restarts a paused monitor (`status = 'live'`); emits `trial_started` and `page_viewed_outside` on the first view only; json `{ok}`.
- Pay buttons (S14): three forms posting to `/api/stripe/checkout` with `monitorId` and `tier`; `paywall_shown` captured in the browser once when the frozen banner mounts.
- Bot button (S15): renders when `bot_state = 'none'` and the state is trial or paid; the code comes from `ensureBotCode` (alerts; imported from `lib/alerts/code.ts`, a seam) called in the server read; the link uses `config.bot.x_user_id`.
- Input classes: `handle` param validated (`normalizeValidHandle`, reserved → 404); `story` param must be a uuid; the beacon json `{monitorId: uuid}` (400 otherwise).

Seams cited: S2, S3, S12, S13, S14, S15, S16, S17, S19, S21, S22.

## 2. Build steps

4. `$vercel:nextjs` then `$web-design-guidelines`: `app/[handle]/page.tsx` (server component, `generateMetadata` with the agent's name and beat, the two-column layout, the tabs, the banner by `monitorState`), `app/[handle]/[story]/page.tsx` (reuses the page component with `storyId`), `app/[handle]/not-found.tsx`, `app/api/view/route.ts` (S13, `maxDuration` default), `posthog` browser captures (`paywall_shown`).

# access.md
Skills: nextjs, supabase, stripe-best-practices, stripe-docs, shadcn, accessibility, instrument-product-analytics

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `app/login/page.tsx`, `app/login/login-form.tsx`, `app/signup/page.tsx`, `app/signup/signup-form.tsx`, `app/forgot-password/*`, `app/auth/confirm/route.ts`, `app/auth/reset-password/*`, `lib/auth/actions.ts`, `lib/auth/content.ts` (S20, new), `lib/auth/oauth.ts` (new), `lib/auth-errors.ts`, `lib/validation.ts`, `lib/user.ts`, `components/auth-shell.tsx`, `components/posthog-user-context.tsx`, `app/checkout/return/page.tsx`, `app/api/stripe/checkout/route.ts`, `app/api/stripe/webhook/route.ts`, `app/api/stripe/portal/route.ts`, `lib/billing/stripe.ts` (the `StripeClient` instance), `lib/billing/prices.ts` (S24), `lib/billing/apply-event.ts`, `lib/billing/bind.ts`.

Contracts:
- Sign-in: the login page gains `Continue with Google` and `Continue with X` buttons (owner, September 24) that call a server action `signInWithProvider('google'|'twitter')` using `supabase.auth.signInWithOAuth` with `redirectTo = origin + '/auth/confirm?next=' + next` (the providers are configured in Supabase; setup.md); email login stays. `/auth/confirm` gains the OAuth code exchange (`exchangeCodeForSession`) and the magic-link path (`verifyOtp` type `magiclink`) both landing on `next` (default `/`); the existing signup-confirmation behavior stays. `next` is validated as a same-origin path (`/` + no `//`). After sign-in: a user with a monitor (`monitors.user_id = uid`) goes to `next` or `/<handle>`; a user with none goes to `/?noagent=1` (the landing shows the box with the no-agent line; owner, September 28). PostHog `identify` on the client after sign-in stays in `PostHogUserContext`.
- Checkout (`POST /api/stripe/checkout`, json `{ monitorId, tier }` zod; tier in hobby|creator|wire): the monitor must be in `frozen`, `lapsed` or `trial` state (paying early is allowed); creates a Checkout Session `mode = 'subscription'`, `line_items = [{ price: await priceIdFor(tier), quantity: 1 }]`, `client_reference_id = monitorId`, `metadata = { monitor_id, tier, handle }`, `customer = monitors.stripe_customer_id` when set (so a lapsed customer reuses the record) else Checkout collects the email, `success_url = origin + '/checkout/return?session_id={CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}'`, `cancel_url = origin + '/' + handle`, `integration_identifier = 'oparax_checkout_' + 8 random letters` (stripe-best-practices), no `payment_method_types`; `track('checkout_started')`; returns `{ url }`.
- Webhook (`POST /api/stripe/webhook`): raw body via `await req.text()`, `stripe.webhooks.constructEvent(body, sig, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET)`; insert `stripe_events (id, type)` → on conflict return 200 (handled once); then `applyEvent`: `checkout.session.completed` with `payment_status = 'paid'` → `bind(monitorId, email, customer, subscription, tier)`; `invoice.paid` → `paid_through = subscription.current_period_end`, `subscription_status = 'active'`, pool reset (S3) and `pool_limit` by tier, `status = 'live'`, `track('payment_succeeded')`; `invoice.payment_failed` → `subscription_status = 'past_due'`, `track('payment_failed')` (the page shows the lapsed copy only after `paid_through` passes, per the owner plan); `customer.subscription.deleted` → `subscription_status = 'canceled'`, `track('subscription_cancelled')` (access ends at `paid_through`). Unknown types → 200. Errors → `processed_at` null, `error` set, 500 so Stripe retries. The webhook route is excluded from `proxy.ts`'s matcher? No: the proxy only refreshes cookies and does not read the body; no change (recorded).
- `bind(...)` (`lib/billing/bind.ts`): `auth.admin.createUser({ email, email_confirm: true })`; on the already-registered error → `rpc('user_id_by_email')`; `monitors` update: `user_id`, `tier`, `stripe_customer_id`, `stripe_subscription_id`, `subscription_status = 'active'`, `paid_through` (from the subscription's `current_period_end`), `pool_limit` by tier, `pool_used = 0`, `pool_period_start = now()`, `cadence` (`every_15m` for wire else `daily`), `status = 'live'`; then `signInWithOtp({ email, options: { emailRedirectTo: origin + '/auth/confirm?next=/' + handle, shouldCreateUser: false } })` through a server client to send the sign-in link (Supabase's SMTP, proven); `track('signin_link_sent')`. A monitor already bound to a different `user_id` is not rebound (logged, `error`).
- Portal (`POST /api/stripe/portal`): session user must own the monitor (RLS-scoped select); `billingPortal.sessions.create({ customer, return_url: origin + '/' + handle + '/settings' })` → `{ url }`.
- Return page (`/checkout/return?session_id=`): retrieves the session (test key), shows the paid copy with the email when `payment_status = 'paid'` and the monitor is bound, else the pending copy; no session → `/`.
- Input classes: every json body through zod; the webhook signature; `next` path validation; provider names as a union.

Seams cited: S3, S4, S14, S19, S20, S24, the RPC `user_id_by_email`.

## 2. Build steps

1. `$stripe:stripe-best-practices` then `$stripe:stripe-docs`: `lib/billing/stripe.ts` (`new Stripe(process.env.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY)`, SDK default API version), `lib/billing/prices.ts` (S24: list prices by `lookup_keys`, create product and price when missing, cache in `config.stripe_prices`).
2. `$supabase:supabase`: `lib/billing/bind.ts`, `lib/billing/apply-event.ts` (the four events), `app/api/stripe/webhook/route.ts` (raw body, `stripe_events` idempotency), `app/api/stripe/checkout/route.ts`, `app/api/stripe/portal/route.ts`.
3. `$vercel:nextjs` then `$vercel:shadcn` then `$accessibility`: `app/checkout/return/page.tsx` (a centered `Card` with the copy), `lib/auth/content.ts` (S20; every string from `landingContent.auth` plus the provider buttons' labels and the no-agent line), `lib/auth/oauth.ts` and the two buttons in `login-form.tsx` (44 px on phones, a `Separator` between email and providers), `/auth/confirm` with the code exchange and magic-link path and the `next` validation, the post-sign-in routing by monitor ownership in `loginAction` and `/auth/confirm`.
4. `$posthog:instrument-product-analytics`: the server events named above; `PostHogUserContext` unchanged except importing nothing from `lib/landing/content.ts`.

## 3. Acceptance journeys

- J16: On localhost with `stripe listen --forward-to localhost:3000/api/stripe/webhook` running and its signing secret in `.env.local` as `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, open the frozen `ottleyai` page (J15), click `Creator, $30 a month`, pay with the test card 4242 4242 4242 4242 and any email you own: the return page shows the paid copy, the email receives a sign-in link, and the page is running again with `tier = creator`, `pool_limit = 3000`, `paid_through` about a month out; `stripe_events` holds the events once each.
- J17: Replay the same webhook event with `stripe events resend <id>`: nothing changes and the row count stays.
- J18: Click the sign-in link: you land on `/ottleyai` signed in, and `/ottleyai/settings` opens; sign out, sign in with Google using a different email: the landing page shows the box with the no-agent line.
- J19: From settings, `Manage subscription` opens the Stripe portal (test mode) and cancelling sets `subscription_status = 'canceled'` while the page keeps running until `paid_through`.

## 4. Owner does at ship

- In the Stripe dashboard (sandbox), Developers, Webhooks: add the endpoint `https://oparax.ai/api/stripe/webhook` with the events `checkout.session.completed`, `invoice.paid`, `invoice.payment_failed`, `customer.subscription.deleted`; copy its signing secret into Vercel as `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (all three environments).
- In the Stripe dashboard, Settings, Billing, Customer portal: save the default configuration once (cancellation on).
- Going live later: swap the sandbox keys for live keys and repeat the webhook step on the live account.

# accounts.md
Skills: vercel-functions, supabase, typesafe-ai

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `lib/accounts/poll.ts`, `lib/accounts/posts.ts`, `app/api/cron/accounts/route.ts`.

Contracts:
- The cron (`accounts`, every minute, S8 claim, `guards()`): selects monitors in trial or paid state (S3) whose `poolOpen` is true, skipping trial monitors when `trialPollingOpen` is false (the credit floor; owner), and whose cadence is due: wire every minute, others when `floor(now minute / 5)` changed since the monitor's last poll (recorded in `monitors.updated_at`? No: a column is needed; use `monitor_accounts.counts_checked_at`? No: the poll time is per monitor; add nothing to the schema: the cron keeps its own `run_claims` row per monitor? Simplest and schema-free: run every monitor whose `id`'s first hex digit modulo 5 equals the current minute modulo 5, so each non-wire monitor polls once per 5 minutes on a fixed slot; wire monitors every minute). For each `monitor_accounts` row with `watched = true`: resolve `x_user_id` once (`xUserByHandle`, $0.010, cached in the row and in `sources.x_user_id`), then `xTimeline(userId, since_id, { max_results: 20, exclude: 'replies,retweets', start_time: first poll → now minus 1 day })` through S5 (bills $0.005 per returned post, once per UTC day); before the call, `consume_pool` is not yet possible (the count is unknown), so the call is made and then `consume_pool(monitorId, returned)` is applied in one statement; when it returns `paused = true`, the monitor's remaining accounts are skipped this run and the page's pool line shows it (`pool_used = pool_limit`). Each returned post becomes an `items` row (`id = 'x:' + post id`, `kind = 'post'`, `source_id = 'x-' + handle` (upserted `sources` row), `url = https://x.com/<handle>/status/<id>`, `title = first 120 characters`, `text = the post's full text with the quoted post appended as [quote] ... [end quote] and link titles`, `published_at`, `outcome = 'full'` when at least 40 characters else `'short'`, `image` from the first photo, `author = { handle, name, x_user_id }`) plus `monitor_items (pending)` for this monitor (S11); `since_id` advances only after the rows are written. Rate: the app's 10,000 per 15 minutes is respected by S5's one-request-per-second queue and the 5-minute slots (owner's reason, recorded: about 666 accounts at every minute).
- The 300-post pool in trial: `pool_limit` 300 (owner); no reset in trial.
- Input classes: the cron header; X responses through the engine's `toPosts` shape (moved into `lib/accounts/posts.ts` as `postsFromTimeline`, reusing the engine's parsing by import from `lib/onboarding/engine.ts`? No: onboarding's `toPosts` is inside `runOnboarding`; this component writes its own small parser for the timeline fields it needs, zod-checked).

Seams cited: S1, S2, S3, S5, S8, S11.

## 2. Build steps

1. `$supabase:supabase`: `lib/accounts/posts.ts` (zod schema of the timeline response: `data[]` with `id, text, created_at, author_id, entities, attachments, referenced_tweets`, `includes.users`, `includes.tweets`, `includes.media`; the mapping to `items` rows above).
2. `$typesafe-ai` (only to note that Jev runs later in feed, not here): `lib/accounts/poll.ts` (the selection, the slots, the user id resolve, the timeline call through S5, `consume_pool`, the writes, `since_id` advance, `monitor_items` pending rows).
3. `$vercel:vercel-functions`: `app/api/cron/accounts/route.ts` (`maxDuration = 300`, claim, guards, `run_completed` with `monitors`, `accounts`, `posts`, `paused`).

## 3. Acceptance journeys

- J20: With `ottleyai` in trial (J13), call `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET" localhost:3000/api/cron/accounts` up to five times a minute apart: `items` gains `x:` rows for the watched accounts that posted in the last day, `pool_used` rises by that count, the ledger shows one `x` row per post at $0.005, and after the feed cron runs the posts show as cards with the handle and name.
- J21: Set `pool_limit = 3` in the table editor and run again: `pool_used` stops at 3, the page's pool line reads `3 of 3 watched posts used this month. Your watched X accounts are paused until ...`, sites keep collecting.

## 4. Owner does at ship

# alerts.md
Skills: nextjs, vercel-functions, supabase, instrument-product-analytics

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `lib/alerts/webhook.ts`, `lib/alerts/commands.ts`, `lib/alerts/send.ts`, `lib/alerts/pack.ts`, `app/api/x/webhook/route.ts`, `app/api/cron/alerts/route.ts`, `scripts/register-x-webhook.mjs` (S18).

Contracts:
- The webhook (`GET /api/x/webhook?crc_token=`): responds `{ response_token: 'sha256=' + base64(HMAC-SHA256(X_CLIENT_SECRET, crc_token)) }` (S25). `POST`: reads the raw body, verifies `x-twitter-webhooks-signature-oauth2` against the same HMAC of the body (401 otherwise), parses the event with zod. The DM event's JSON shape is a named build-time check: read docs.x.com's Activity API event reference (`dm.received` or the `direct_message_events` shape) from the X docs pages fetched at build with the shipped docs only, and write the zod schema from it; candidate shapes: `{ event_type: 'dm.received', data: { event_id, sender_id, text, created_at } }` or the legacy `direct_message_events[]` array; the parser accepts whichever the docs specify and logs the raw payload for the owner on a shape it cannot read (`handled = 'logged'`). Every event is inserted into `dm_events` by `event_id` (on conflict: 200, nothing else); a received DM bills $0.010 (S1, `kind = 'dm_received'`). Then `commands.ts`: the text trimmed and upper-cased; a 10-character code matching `monitors.bot_code` binds when `sender_x_user_id = monitors.x_user_id` (`bot_x_user_id = sender`, `bot_state = 'active'`, `track('bot_connected')`, reply `Connected...`), else reply `This code belongs to another X account.`; `STOP` → `bot_state = 'stopped'`; `PAUSE` → `'paused'`; `RESUME` → `'active'`; the reply DMs go through `xSendDm` (each $0.015). Anything else: `handled = 'logged'`. A message from the bot's own id is ignored.
- The sender cron (`alerts`, every minute, claim, `guards()`): for each monitor with `bot_state = 'active'` and state trial or paid (S3): due when cadence `every_15m` and the minute is a multiple of 15, or cadence `daily` and `nextDailySlot(m, now) <= now` and `last_alert_at` is before that slot. Stories to send: `stories` with `status = 'written'`, `alerted_at is null`, `opened_at > monitors.bot connected time` (use `dm_events.received_at` of the `activated` event, or simply `stories.opened_at >= the activation time stored`: store it in `monitors.updated_at`? Store nothing new: use the `dm_events` row's `received_at` where `handled = 'activated'` for this monitor, newest), ordered by `opened_at`, at most `DM_STORIES = 10` per DM (assistant's default); the rest wait for the next slot. Nothing to send → no DM, `last_alert_at` unchanged for `every_15m`, set for `daily` (the day's slot passed). Before sending: today's `deliveries` with `state = 'sent'` counted across all monitors; at 1,440 the run stops (`delivery_held` events). Per story a `deliveries` row `state = 'sending'` is inserted (existing row → skip that story: never twice); one `xSendDm(bot_x_user_id, packed text)`; `ok` → every included story `sent`, `dm_event_id`, `stories.alerted_at = now()`, `last_alert_at`, `track('story_alerted')` per story, and S1 bills the send; `ok: false, uncertain: false` → `state = 'failed'`, `tries + 1` (a second failure leaves `failed`; the story is not retried a third time); `uncertain: true` → `state = 'held'` and `track('delivery_held')`; held rows are never retried by code (owner plan). The per-recipient limit of 15 per 15 minutes cannot be reached (one DM per slot).
- `pack.ts`: the DM text from shared.md section 5 (`Oparax: {n} new stories for you` then per story headline, first fact, link `https://oparax.ai/{handle}/{storyId}`), cut to X's 10,000-character DM limit by dropping stories from the end (they go to the next slot).
- `scripts/register-x-webhook.mjs` (S18): with `X_BEARER_TOKEN`: `POST /2/webhooks { url: 'https://oparax.ai/api/x/webhook' }` (X performs the CRC at once), then with `X_BOT_BEARER_TOKEN`: `POST /2/account_activity/webhooks/{id}/subscriptions/all`; prints the webhook id and each status; lists existing webhooks first and reuses one with the same URL. The subscription call's exact path and token are a named build-time check against docs.x.com's Activity API reference; if the bot token is refused for the subscription, the script prints the refusal and the owner-at-ship list names the console alternative.
- Input classes: the CRC query (`crc_token` required), the signature header, the event body (zod), the cron header.

Seams cited: S1, S2, S3, S5, S8, S12, S15, S18, S25.

## 2. Build steps

1. `$vercel:nextjs`: `lib/alerts/webhook.ts` (CRC, signature, the zod event schema from the docs check, `dm_events` insert) and `app/api/x/webhook/route.ts` (GET and POST; the POST body read as text before parsing).
2. `$supabase:supabase`: `lib/alerts/commands.ts` (bind, STOP, PAUSE, RESUME, replies through S5).
3. `lib/alerts/pack.ts` and `lib/alerts/send.ts` (the sender flow above, `deliveries` states, the 1,440 cap), `$posthog:instrument-product-analytics` for `story_alerted`, `delivery_held`, `bot_connected`, `bot_paused`, `bot_stopped`.
4. `$vercel:vercel-functions`: `app/api/cron/alerts/route.ts` (`maxDuration = 300`, claim, guards, `run_completed` with `dms`, `stories`, `held`, `failed`) and `scripts/register-x-webhook.mjs`.

## 3. Acceptance journeys

- J22: On the owner's own agent page (built from the landing box with `farzanmrz`), in trial, click `Get alerts on X`, send the prefilled code from @farzanmrz to @oparax_ai; on localhost the webhook cannot receive, so instead insert a `dm_events` row by hand with the code and your X id and call the command handler's route? No: the honest localhost proof is the sender: set `bot_state = 'active'` and `bot_x_user_id = 1633203438` in the table editor, then `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET" "localhost:3000/api/cron/alerts"` at the daily slot (set `alert_hour` to the current UTC hour and `alert_timezone` null): one real DM arrives in @farzanmrz's inbox with the headline, first fact and link of each unalerted story (a real DM, about 1.5 cents; the owner's own account, authorized September 28); `deliveries` rows are `sent`, `stories.alerted_at` set.
- J23: Run the cron again in the same slot: no second DM; the next day's slot sends only stories opened since.
- J24: At ship, after `node scripts/register-x-webhook.mjs` succeeds, reply STOP to the bot from @farzanmrz: `dm_events` records it, `bot_state = 'stopped'`, the bot answers `Alerts stopped...`; RESUME turns it back on.

## 4. Owner does at ship

## 1. Files and contracts

Files owned: `app/[handle]/settings/page.tsx`, `lib/settings/actions.ts` ("use server"), `lib/settings/sources.ts` (the trust logic the actions call), `lib/settings/accounts.ts`, `lib/settings/content.ts`, `components/settings/source-editor.tsx`, `components/settings/account-picker.tsx`, `components/settings/alert-settings.tsx`, `components/settings/digest-switches.tsx`, `components/settings/billing-card.tsx`.

Screen, one line: `/[handle]/settings` for the signed-in owner of the page only: `Tabs` (`Sources`, `X accounts`, `Alerts`, `Digests`, `Billing`), each a `Card` (shadcn's settings recipe); `Sources`: a `Table` of sources (name, focus, `Switch` "Do not filter", `Button` remove with an `AlertDialog`), an `Input` and `Button` to add a source by URL; `X accounts`: a `Table` of the accounts with a `Checkbox` "Watch", posts per day, the running total line `About {n} posts a day across watched accounts, {m} of {limit} used this month`, one `Alert` (amber) when a single account's posts per day times 10 exceeds the remaining pool (`would empty the pool in under 10 days`; owner); `Alerts`: a `Select` for the hour (0 to 23 in the person's timezone shown by name), the cadence shown (not editable: it follows the tier), the bot state line; `Digests`: two `Switch`es and, for GitHub, a `Table` of followed repos with a threshold `Input` and add/remove; `Billing`: the tier, `paid_through`, `Manage subscription` (POST portal). All controls 44 px on phones, labels on every control, errors inline with `role="alert"` (`accessibility`).

Contracts:
- Ownership: the page reads the monitor with the RLS-scoped server client (`monitors` select policy) and redirects to `/login?next=/<handle>/settings` when signed out, shows the wrong-account copy when the row is not visible; every action re-checks with the RLS client (`select id from monitors where handle = ... and user_id = auth.uid()`) before using the admin client for the deny-all tables (Ownership first; trust logic in `sources.ts`/`accounts.ts`, not in the "use server" file). Settings exist only in the paid state (S3 `paid` or `lapsed`), else the page shows the frozen copy with the pay buttons? No: settings are after checkout; a trial page has no owner, so the ownership check already excludes it.
- Actions (each returns `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, error }`): `addSource(handle, url)`: validates the URL (public http(s)), runs `discoverSource(url)` (collect owns `lib/sources/discovery.ts`; its exported discovery function is a seam the settings action calls, read-only), admits a feed or a listing page that yields at least 5 article-shaped links, upserts `sources` (`id = 'web-' + host + '-' + sha1(url).slice(0,8)`, `kind`, `target`, `name = the site's title or host`, `added_by person` on `monitor_sources`), `track('source_added')`; `removeSource(handle, sourceId)`: `removed_at = now()`, `track('source_removed')`; `setNoFilter(handle, sourceId, on)`; `setWatched(handle, accountHandle, on)` (`track('account_watch_changed')`); `refreshCounts(handle)`: for each account without `counts_checked_at` in the last 7 days, `xCounts` (S5, $0.005 each, billed to the monitor) → `posts_per_day`, `counts_checked_at`; `setAlertHour(handle, hour, timezone)` (`alert_timezone` from a list of IANA names via `Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')`); `setDigest(handle, kind, on)` (`digest_github`/`digest_product_hunt`; on first GitHub switch-on the topics are computed by digests' cron, not here); `followRepo(handle, repo, threshold)` / `unfollowRepo` (`followed_repos`; `repo` validated as `owner/name`); `openPortal(handle)` → the access route's url.
- Input classes: every action's arguments through zod; URLs through the SSRF-safe discovery; handles through `normalizeValidHandle`.

Seams cited: S3, S4, S5, S14, S17, S19, S22, the discovery export of collect (`discoverSource` in `lib/sources/discovery.ts`, existing signature kept).

## 2. Build steps

1. `$supabase:supabase`: `lib/settings/sources.ts` and `lib/settings/accounts.ts` (the trust logic: ownership check helper `ownedMonitor(handle)` with the RLS client, the source admission, the counts refresh through S5), `lib/settings/actions.ts` (thin "use server" wrappers returning result shapes), `$posthog:instrument-product-analytics` events.
2. `$vercel:shadcn` then `$accessibility` then `$web-design-guidelines`: `lib/settings/content.ts` and the five components (`useActionState` forms, inline errors, `AlertDialog` for remove, 44 px targets).
3. `$vercel:nextjs`: `app/[handle]/settings/page.tsx` (server component: the ownership read, `Tabs`, `SiteHeader`/`SiteFooter`, redirect and wrong-account states).

## 3. Acceptance journeys

Contracts:
- The cron (`digests`, daily 06:00 UTC, claim, `guards()`, `maxDuration = 800`): for each monitor in the paid state with a switch on (S3): (1) topics: when `github_topics` is null, one Luna fast call (`openai/gpt-6-luna-fast`, structured output `{ topics: string[] (3 to 8), keywords: string[] }`) over the beat and the brief, with a `prompt-format` prompt in `prompts.ts`; ledgered (`kind = 'topics'`) and captured; (2) GitHub (`GITHUB_TOKEN`, free): `GET /search/repositories?q=topic:<topic> created:><7 days ago> stars:>100&sort=stars` per topic (the "new" pass; 100 stars the assistant's floor from the #136 brief) and `q=topic:<topic> pushed:><7 days ago> stars:>1000&sort=updated` (the "rising" pass; rising is measured as stars gained this week from a second read of `stargazers_count` stored in `digest_items.description`? No: keep it simple, the assistant's choice: "rising" = repos with over 1,000 stars pushed this week that the person has not seen; a star-velocity measure is a later refinement), at most 30 candidates a day per monitor; each candidate `{ name, url, description, stars, created_at }`; (3) Jev fit (S6, `kind = 'digest'`) with the question "Does `repo` belong to what the person wants monitored?" over `{ beat, person: { brief }, repo: { name, description, topics } }`, line 0.5 (the fit line, owner); survivors become `digest_items` rows (`kind = 'github_repo'`, `why_now = '{stars} stars, created {n} days ago'` or `'{stars} stars, active this week'`, `description = the repo's description, first 2 sentences`), unique per monitor and repo; (4) Product Hunt (`PRODUCT_HUNT_TOKEN`, GraphQL `posts(postedAfter: yesterday, order: VOTES, first: 30)` with `name, tagline, description, url, votesCount, topics`): the same fit question over `{ beat, person, launch }`, rows `kind = 'product_hunt'`, `why_now = '#{rank} on Product Hunt today, {votes} votes'`; (5) thresholds: for each `followed_repos` row, `GET /repos/{repo}` → `stars`; when `stars >= threshold` and `crossed_at is null` → `crossed_at = now()` and a `digest_items` row `kind = 'star_threshold'`, `why_now = 'Passed {threshold} stars ({stars})'`. Digest cards are code-written (no writer model; the owner plan). They appear on the page's digest block (S17) and ride in the next daily DM as one line each? No (assistant's choice, stated): digest items do not enter DMs this build; the DM is stories only (owner: "one DM per story"). The page shows them.

Seams cited: S1, S2, S3, S6, S7, S8, S17.

2. `$supabase:supabase`: `lib/digests/github.ts`, `lib/digests/product-hunt.ts` (fetches with zod, no SSRF concern: fixed hosts), `lib/digests/thresholds.ts`.

# Cross-review, turn 1 (Fable to Astra), detailed plan

My sealed draft is now visible to you (the component table, shared.md, twelve slices). Compare in the protocol's order: the cut, then the seams and numbers, then the slices. Under 1,500 words, numbered, each point agree / disagree with evidence / a specific alternative.

## The cut: where we agree
Same 12 ids, same single `migrations: yes`, same "table and route seams, not function imports" shape. I take from you: `landing` depends on `access` as well (the auth copy move), and `lib/supabase/admin.ts` joins `core`'s files for the missing `server-only` import.

## Where I hold my design, and why (each a real difference)
1. **Size.** Your schema has 31 tables and 21 RPCs (spend_calls, spend_allocations, provider_resources, pool_periods, pool_debits, rate_buckets, checkout_attempts, bot_challenges, dm_batches, operational_events, source_candidates, item_sources, build_runs, build_events ...), plus `/ops`, three extra crons (`onboarding`, `billing`, `contact`, `maintenance` every 5 minutes) and a per-call reservation protocol (`reserve_call`, `dispatch_call`, `settle_call`). Each component gets one 60-minute build (orchestration.md) and files stay under about 400 lines (AGENTS.md, Low reader load; Laziness: no abstraction with a single caller). The data model alone would not finish in an hour, and every worker would carry the reservation dance. Mine: 19 tables, 5 RPCs, the ledger as one table, the build-level reservation as one RPC. I hold the smaller schema and ask you to name the specific owner ruling or failure each extra table prevents that mine does not; where you can, I add the column, not the table.
2. **Per-call reservations.** The owner's rule is "a cost row written before every paid X or model call" and "pools that stop at zero in the same write". Mine writes the row before the call with the upper bound (`usd_reserved`, `settled = false`) and settles it in place after; the build-level $3 reservation and the pool RPC are the admission checks. I concede one thing from you: `cost || marketCost` is a bug; S7 returns `{ charged, market }` and zero charged is zero. Do you accept row-before-call-then-settle as satisfying the ruling without `dispatch_call`?
3. **Onboarding durability.** You add a `build_runs` table, leases and an onboarding cron. I keep the run in `monitors` (`build_step`, `build_log`, saved `profile` and `posts`) and add one small cron `app/api/cron/builds` (every 5 minutes, onboarding's file) that resumes a `building` monitor whose last log entry is older than 10 minutes, at most 2 attempts (`build_tries` column), then `failed`. Same guarantee, two columns and one route.
4. **Brief shape.** I accept your structured PersonBrief: `brief jsonb` with `summary`, `interests[]`, `languages[]`, `topic_terms[]`, written in the same Luna call; digests then use `topic_terms` and drop their own Luna call (your point, accepted).
5. **Owner exclusion.** The owner ruled "his signed-in session is excluded; no IP lists". Mine excludes `OWNER_EMAILS` sessions only. You add the creating browser and an IP HMAC; the IP part contradicts "no IP lists" and the creating-browser part changes his rule (a stranger who builds their own page and looks at it is the first outside view: that is the product). I hold.
6. **Checkout timing.** You refuse checkout before the trial ends; I allowed paying early. The owner plan says "No card before day seven", so you are right: checkout only in `frozen` or `lapsed`. Accepted.
7. **Login.** I accept "Email me a sign-in link" on the login page (payers have no password) beside the existing password form and the two provider buttons; I do not delete the password path or the signup page (unrequested).
8. **Activation code.** You hash it with a 30-minute expiry and rate limits; mine is a plain 10-character code that only binds when the sender's immutable X id equals the page's `x_user_id`. The id check is the security; the hash adds nothing a stranger can exploit. I hold.
9. **Trial watch start.** Accepted: the first poll of a watched account uses `start_time = trial_started_at`, never a day of backfill.
10. **DM packing at 9,000 code points** (yours) instead of 10,000 characters: accepted.
11. **Stripe events.** Accepted: also handle `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` (same as completed when paid) and `customer.subscription.updated` (refresh `paid_through`, `subscription_status`, `cancel_at_period_end`). `STRIPE_ALLOW_LIVE` is not added: only sandbox keys exist and going live is an owner key swap.
12. **`/ops`, rate buckets, operational_events, a mail drain cron, a billing cron.** Not built: PostHog is the one dashboard (owner), the four Slack alerts are the owner's list, Stripe retries webhooks itself, and Contact sends inline with the row kept on failure. I hold.
13. **Jev boolean vs Noul.** The existing Gateway call (`type: "boolean"`, `answers.<id>.probability`) ran on September 27 for Liam; the client keeps that contract. No adapter check needed. Do you have evidence the Gateway evaluate endpoint changed since?
14. **`items.id` as a text hash** (R1) and `monitor_items` keyed by it, versus your uuid plus `identity_key`: mine has one fewer column and the same uniqueness. I hold.

## Ask
Review my sealed draft the same way: name any file two of my components would both write, any dependency I lack, any seam a slice cites that shared.md does not define (or defines differently), any number without attribution, and any owner ruling I got wrong. Then say which of 1 to 14 you concede and which you hold.

# Accessibility (a11y)

Comprehensive accessibility guidelines based on WCAG 2.2 and Lighthouse accessibility audits. Goal: make content usable by everyone, including people with disabilities.

## Evidence-led audit workflow

When a rendered page is available:

1. Run a live Lighthouse Accessibility audit when that capability is available; with Chrome DevTools MCP, use `lighthouse_audit`. Use mobile navigation mode for a general public page or snapshot mode when reloading would lose authenticated or user-created state.
2. Use failed audit nodes to localize the relevant component or template instead of searching the whole repository for generic patterns.
3. Inspect a rendered accessibility-tree snapshot for names, roles, states, landmarks, and heading structure; with Chrome DevTools MCP, use `take_snapshot`. Exercise the affected flow with the keyboard.
4. Fix the source, then re-run the same audit and manual interaction.

If the live tools are unavailable, use Lighthouse CLI or axe for automated coverage and complete the same manual checks. Automated tools detect only a subset of accessibility barriers: a score of 100 is not WCAG conformance, and a low score does not replace issue-level evidence.

## WCAG Principles: POUR

| Principle | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| **P**erceivable | Content can be perceived through different senses |
| **O**perable | Interface can be operated by all users |
| **U**nderstandable | Content and interface are understandable |
| **R**obust | Content works with assistive technologies |

## Conformance levels

| Level | Requirement | Target |
|-------|-------------|--------|
| **A** | Minimum accessibility | Must pass |
| **AA** | Standard compliance | Should pass (legal requirement in many jurisdictions) |
| **AAA** | Enhanced accessibility | Nice to have |

---

## Perceivable

### Text alternatives (1.1)

**Images require alt text:**
```html
<!-- ❌ Missing alt -->
<img src="chart.png">

<!-- ✅ Descriptive alt -->
<img src="chart.png" alt="Bar chart showing 40% increase in Q3 sales">

<!-- ✅ Decorative image (empty alt) -->
<img src="decorative-border.png" alt="" role="presentation">

<!-- ✅ Complex image with longer description -->
<figure>
  <img src="infographic.png" alt="2024 market trends infographic" 
       aria-describedby="infographic-desc">
  <figcaption id="infographic-desc">
    <!-- Detailed description -->
  </figcaption>
</figure>
```

**Icon buttons need accessible names:**
```html
<!-- ❌ No accessible name -->
<button><svg><!-- menu icon --></svg></button>

<!-- ✅ Using aria-label -->
<button aria-label="Open menu">
  <svg aria-hidden="true"><!-- menu icon --></svg>
</button>

<!-- ✅ Using visually hidden text -->
<button>
  <svg aria-hidden="true"><!-- menu icon --></svg>
  <span class="visually-hidden">Open menu</span>
</button>
```

**Visually hidden class:**
```css
.visually-hidden {
  position: absolute;
  width: 1px;
  height: 1px;
  padding: 0;
  margin: -1px;
  overflow: hidden;
  clip: rect(0, 0, 0, 0);
  white-space: nowrap;
  border: 0;
}
```

### Color contrast (1.4.3, 1.4.6)

| Text Size | AA minimum | AAA enhanced |
|-----------|------------|--------------|
| Normal text (< 18px / < 14px bold) | 4.5:1 | 7:1 |
| Large text (≥ 18px / ≥ 14px bold) | 3:1 | 4.5:1 |
| UI components & graphics | 3:1 | 3:1 |

```css
/* ❌ Low contrast (2.5:1) */
.low-contrast {
  color: #999;
  background: #fff;
}

/* ✅ Sufficient contrast (7:1) */
.high-contrast {
  color: #333;
  background: #fff;
}

/* ✅ Focus states need contrast too (3:1 against background, WCAG 1.4.11) */
:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid currentColor;
  outline-offset: 2px;
}
```

**Don't rely on color alone:**
```html
<!-- ❌ Only color indicates error -->
<input class="error-border">
<style>.error-border { border-color: red; }</style>

<!-- ✅ Color + icon + text -->
<div class="field-error">
  <input aria-invalid="true" aria-describedby="email-error">
  <span id="email-error" class="error-message">
    <svg aria-hidden="true"><!-- error icon --></svg>
    Please enter a valid email address
  </span>
</div>
```

### Media alternatives (1.2)

```html
<!-- Video with captions -->
<video controls>
  <source src="video.mp4" type="video/mp4">
  <track kind="captions" src="captions.vtt" srclang="en" label="English" default>
  <track kind="descriptions" src="descriptions.vtt" srclang="en" label="Descriptions">
</video>

<!-- Audio with transcript -->
<audio controls>
  <source src="podcast.mp3" type="audio/mp3">
</audio>
<details>
  <summary>Transcript</summary>
  <p>Full transcript text...</p>
</details>
```

---

## Operable

### Keyboard accessible (2.1)

**All functionality must be keyboard accessible.** Prefer native interactive elements — `<button>`, `<a href>`, and form controls handle Enter/Space activation, focus, and assistive-tech semantics for free. Only add manual keyboard handling when you cannot use a native element.

```html
<!-- ❌ Non-interactive element with click only: not focusable, no keyboard activation -->
<div class="card" onclick="handleAction()">Open</div>

<!-- ✅ Best: use a native button -->
<button type="button" onclick="handleAction()">Open</button>
```

```javascript
// ✅ When you MUST use a non-interactive element (e.g. div with role="button"),
// make it focusable AND handle keyboard activation. Do NOT add this to a native
// <button> — Enter/Space already fire click, so you'd double-trigger.
element.setAttribute('role', 'button');
element.setAttribute('tabindex', '0');
element.addEventListener('click', handleAction);
element.addEventListener('keydown', (e) => {
  if (e.key === 'Enter' || e.key === ' ') {
    e.preventDefault();
    handleAction();
  }
});
```

**No keyboard traps.** Users must be able to Tab into and out of every component. Use the [modal focus trap pattern](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#modal-focus-trap) for dialogs—the native `<dialog>` element handles this automatically.

### Focus visible (2.4.7)

```css
/* ❌ Never remove focus outlines */
*:focus { outline: none; }

/* ✅ Use :focus-visible for keyboard-only focus */
:focus {
  outline: none;
}

:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid currentColor; /* inherits text color → already contrast-checked */
  outline-offset: 2px;
}

/* ✅ Or pick a brand color and verify ≥3:1 contrast against every background it lands on */
button:focus-visible {
  box-shadow: 0 0 0 3px rgba(0, 95, 204, 0.5);
}
```

### Focus not obscured (2.4.11) — new in 2.2

When an element receives keyboard focus, it must not be entirely hidden by other author-created content such as sticky headers, footers, or overlapping panels. At Level AAA (2.4.12), no part of the focused element may be hidden.

```css
/* ✅ Account for sticky headers when scrolling to focused elements */
:target {
  scroll-margin-top: 80px;
}

/* ✅ Ensure focused items clear fixed/sticky bars */
:focus {
  scroll-margin-top: 80px;
  scroll-margin-bottom: 60px;
}
```

### Skip links (2.4.1)

Provide a skip link so keyboard users can bypass repetitive navigation. See the [skip link pattern](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#skip-link) for full markup and styles.

### Target size (2.5.8) — new in 2.2

Interactive targets must be at least **24 × 24 CSS pixels** (AA). Exceptions: inline text links, elements where the browser controls the size, and targets where a 24px circle centered on the bounding box does not overlap another target.

```css
/* ✅ Minimum target size */
button,
[role="button"],
input[type="checkbox"] + label,
input[type="radio"] + label {
  min-width: 24px;
  min-height: 24px;
}

/* ✅ Comfortable target size (recommended 44×44) */
.touch-target {
  min-width: 44px;
  min-height: 44px;
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
}
```

### Dragging movements (2.5.7) — new in 2.2

Any action that requires dragging must have a single-pointer alternative (e.g., buttons, inputs). See the [dragging movements pattern](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#dragging-movements) for a sortable-list example.

### Timing (2.2)

```javascript
// Allow users to extend time limits
function showSessionWarning() {
  const modal = createModal({
    title: 'Session Expiring',
    content: 'Your session will expire in 2 minutes.',
    actions: [
      { label: 'Extend session', action: extendSession },
      { label: 'Log out', action: logout }
    ],
    timeout: 120000
  });
}
```

### Motion (2.3)

```css
/* Respect reduced motion preference */
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  *,
  *::before,
  *::after {
    animation-duration: 0.01ms !important;
    animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
    transition-duration: 0.01ms !important;
    scroll-behavior: auto !important;
  }
}
```

---

## Understandable

### Page language (3.1.1)

```html
<!-- ❌ No language specified -->
<html>

<!-- ✅ Language specified -->
<html lang="en">

<!-- ✅ Language changes within page -->
<p>The French word for hello is <span lang="fr">bonjour</span>.</p>
```

### Consistent navigation (3.2.3)

```html
<!-- Navigation should be consistent across pages -->
<nav aria-label="Main">
  <ul>
    <li><a href="/" aria-current="page">Home</a></li>
    <li><a href="/products">Products</a></li>
    <li><a href="/about">About</a></li>
  </ul>
</nav>
```

### Consistent help (3.2.6) — new in 2.2

If a help mechanism (contact info, chat widget, FAQ link, self-help option) is repeated across multiple pages, it must appear in the **same relative order** each time. Users who rely on consistent placement shouldn't have to hunt for help on every page.

### Form labels (3.3.2)

Every input needs a programmatically associated label. See the [form labels pattern](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#form-labels) for explicit, implicit, and instructional examples.

### Error handling (3.3.1, 3.3.3)

Announce errors to screen readers with `role="alert"` or `aria-live`, set `aria-invalid="true"` on invalid fields, and focus the first error on submit. See the [error handling pattern](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#error-handling) for full markup and JS.

### Redundant entry (3.3.7) — new in 2.2

Don't force users to re-enter information they already provided in the same session. Auto-populate from earlier steps, or let users select from previously entered values. Exceptions: security re-confirmation and content that has expired.

```html
<!-- ✅ Auto-fill shipping address from billing -->
<fieldset>
  <legend>Shipping address</legend>
  <label>
    <input type="checkbox" id="same-as-billing" checked>
    Same as billing address
  </label>
  <!-- Fields auto-populated when checked -->
</fieldset>
```

### Accessible authentication (3.3.8) — new in 2.2

Login flows must not rely on cognitive function tests (e.g., remembering a password, solving a puzzle) unless at least one of:
- A copy-paste or autofill mechanism is available
- An alternative method exists (e.g., passkey, SSO, email link)
- The test uses object recognition or personal content (AA only; AAA removes this exception)

```html
<!-- ✅ Allow paste in password fields -->
<input type="password" id="password" autocomplete="current-password">

<!-- ✅ Offer passwordless alternatives -->
<button type="button">Sign in with passkey</button>
<button type="button">Email me a login link</button>
```

---

## Robust

### ARIA usage (4.1.2)

**Prefer native elements:**
```html
<!-- ❌ ARIA role on div -->
<div role="button" tabindex="0">Click me</div>

<!-- ✅ Native button -->
<button>Click me</button>

<!-- ❌ ARIA checkbox -->
<div role="checkbox" aria-checked="false">Option</div>

<!-- ✅ Native checkbox -->
<label><input type="checkbox"> Option</label>
```

**When ARIA is needed,** use the correct roles and states. See the [ARIA tabs pattern](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#aria-tabs) for a complete tablist example.

### Live regions (4.1.3)

Use `aria-live` regions to announce dynamic content changes without moving focus. See the [live regions pattern](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#live-regions-and-notifications) for markup and a `showNotification()` helper.

---

## Testing checklist

### Automated testing

Prefer a live Lighthouse audit that returns failing rendered nodes directly to the agent. With Chrome DevTools MCP, this is `lighthouse_audit`. Otherwise:

```bash
# Lighthouse accessibility audit
npx lighthouse https://example.com --only-categories=accessibility

# axe-core
npm install @axe-core/cli -g
axe https://example.com
```

### Manual testing

- [ ] **Keyboard navigation:** Tab through entire page, use Enter/Space to activate
- [ ] **Screen reader:** Test with VoiceOver (Mac), NVDA (Windows), or TalkBack (Android)
- [ ] **Zoom:** Content usable at 200% zoom
- [ ] **High contrast:** Test with Windows High Contrast Mode
- [ ] **Reduced motion:** Test with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`
- [ ] **Focus order:** Logical and follows visual order
- [ ] **Target size:** Interactive elements meet 24×24px minimum

See the [screen reader commands reference](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md#screen-reader-commands) for VoiceOver and NVDA shortcuts.

---

## Common issues by impact

### Critical (fix immediately)
1. Missing form labels
2. Missing image alt text
3. Insufficient color contrast
4. Keyboard traps
5. No focus indicators

### Serious (fix before launch)
1. Missing page language
2. Missing heading structure
3. Non-descriptive link text
4. Auto-playing media
5. Missing skip links

### Moderate (fix soon)
1. Missing ARIA labels on icons
2. Inconsistent navigation
3. Missing error identification
4. Timing without controls
5. Missing landmark regions

## References

- [WCAG 2.2 Quick Reference](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/quickref/)
- [WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/)
- [Deque axe Rules](https://dequeuniversity.com/rules/axe/)
- [Web Quality Audit](../web-quality-audit/SKILL.md)
- [WCAG criteria reference](references/WCAG.md)
- [Accessibility code patterns](references/A11Y-PATTERNS.md)

</skill>

name: ai-elements
description: Build AI chat interfaces using ai-elements components — conversations, messages, tool displays, prompt inputs, and more. Use when the user wants to build a chatbot, AI assistant UI, or any AI-powered chat interface.
---

# AI Elements

[AI Elements](https://www.npmjs.com/package/ai-elements) is a component library and custom registry built on top of [shadcn/ui](https://ui.shadcn.com/) to help you build AI-native applications faster. It provides pre-built components like conversations, messages and more.

Installing AI Elements is straightforward and can be done in a couple of ways. You can use the dedicated CLI command for the fastest setup, or integrate via the standard shadcn/ui CLI if you've already adopted shadcn's workflow.

> **IMPORTANT:** Run all CLI commands using the project's package runner: `npx ai-elements@latest`, `pnpm dlx ai-elements@latest`, or `bunx --bun ai-elements@latest` — based on the project's `packageManager`. Examples below use `npx ai-elements@latest` but substitute the correct runner for the project.

## Prerequisites

Before installing AI Elements, make sure your environment meets the following requirements:

- [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/en/download/), version 18 or later
- A [Next.js](https://nextjs.org/) project with the [AI SDK](https://ai-sdk.dev/) installed.
- [shadcn/ui](https://ui.shadcn.com/) installed in your project. If you don't have it installed, running any install command will automatically install it for you.
- We also highly recommend using the [AI Gateway](https://vercel.com/docs/ai-gateway) and adding `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` to your `env.local` so you don't have to use an API key from every provider. AI Gateway also gives $5 in usage per month so you can experiment with models. You can obtain an API key [here](https://vercel.com/d?to=%2F%5Bteam%5D%2F%7E%2Fai%2Fapi-keys&title=Get%20your%20AI%20Gateway%20key).

## Installing Components

You can install AI Elements components using either the AI Elements CLI or the shadcn/ui CLI. Both achieve the same result: adding the selected component’s code and any needed dependencies to your project.

The CLI will download the component’s code and integrate it into your project’s directory (usually under your components folder). By default, AI Elements components are added to the `@/components/ai-elements/` directory (or whatever folder you’ve configured in your shadcn components settings).

After running the command, you should see a confirmation in your terminal that the files were added. You can then proceed to use the component in your code.

## Usage

Once an AI Elements component is installed, you can import it and use it in your application like any other React component. The components are added as part of your codebase (not hidden in a library), so the usage feels very natural.

## Example

After installing AI Elements components, you can use them in your application like any other React component. For example:

```tsx title="conversation.tsx"
"use client";

import {
  Message,
  MessageContent,
  MessageResponse,
} from "@/components/ai-elements/message";
import { useChat } from "@ai-sdk/react";

const Example = () => {
  const { messages } = useChat();

  return (
    <>
      {messages.map(({ role, parts }, index) => (
        <Message from={role} key={index}>
          <MessageContent>
            {parts.map((part, i) => {
              switch (part.type) {
                case "text":
                  return (
                    <MessageResponse key={`${role}-${i}`}>
                      {part.text}
                    </MessageResponse>
                  );
              }
            })}
          </MessageContent>
        </Message>
      ))}
    </>
  );
};

export default Example;
```

In the example above, we import the `Message` component from our AI Elements directory and include it in our JSX. Then, we compose the component with the `MessageContent` and `MessageResponse` subcomponents. You can style or configure the component just as you would if you wrote it yourself – since the code lives in your project, you can even open the component file to see how it works or make custom modifications.

## Extensibility

All AI Elements components take as many primitive attributes as possible. For example, the `Message` component extends `HTMLAttributes<HTMLDivElement>`, so you can pass any props that a `div` supports. This makes it easy to extend the component with your own styles or functionality.

## Customization

After installation, no additional setup is needed. The component’s styles (Tailwind CSS classes) and scripts are already integrated. You can start interacting with the component in your app immediately.

For example, if you'd like to remove the rounding on `Message`, you can go to `components/ai-elements/message.tsx` and remove `rounded-lg` as follows:

```tsx title="components/ai-elements/message.tsx" highlight="8"
export const MessageContent = ({
  children,
  className,
  ...props
}: MessageContentProps) => (
  <div
    className={cn(
      "flex flex-col gap-2 text-sm text-foreground",
      "group-[.is-user]:bg-primary group-[.is-user]:text-primary-foreground group-[.is-user]:px-4 group-[.is-user]:py-3",
      className
    )}
    {...props}
  >
    <div className="is-user:dark">{children}</div>
  </div>
);
```

## Troubleshooting

### Why are my components not styled?

Make sure your project is configured correctly for shadcn/ui in Tailwind 4 - this means having a `globals.css` file that imports Tailwind and includes the shadcn/ui base styles.

### I ran the AI Elements CLI but nothing was added to my project

Double-check that:

- Your current working directory is the root of your project (where `package.json` lives).
- Your components.json file (if using shadcn-style config) is set up correctly.
- You’re using the latest version of the AI Elements CLI:

```bash title="Terminal"
npx ai-elements@latest
```

If all else fails, feel free to open an [issue on GitHub](https://github.com/vercel/ai-elements/issues).

### Theme switching doesn’t work — my app stays in light mode

Ensure your app is using the same data-theme system that shadcn/ui and AI Elements expect. The default implementation toggles a data-theme attribute on the `<html>` element. Make sure your tailwind.config.js is using class or data- selectors accordingly.

### The component imports fail with “module not found”

Check the file exists. If it does, make sure your `tsconfig.json` has a proper paths alias for `@/` i.e.

```json title="tsconfig.json"
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "baseUrl": ".",
    "paths": {
      "@/*": ["./*"]
    }
  }
}
```

### My AI coding assistant can't access AI Elements components

1. Verify your config file syntax is valid JSON.
2. Check that the file path is correct for your AI tool.
3. Restart your coding assistant after making changes.
4. Ensure you have a stable internet connection.

### Still stuck?

If none of these answers help, open an [issue on GitHub](https://github.com/vercel/ai-elements/issues) and someone will be happy to assist.

## Available Components

See the `references/` folder for detailed documentation on each component.

</skill>

name: beautiful-shadows
description: Apply exact Tailwind arbitrary shadow utilities for polished, layered neutral elevation. Use when compact cards, controls, panels, popovers, hero media, feature callouts, or modal-like containers need refined shadows without default Tailwind shadow scales or colored tinting.
---

# Beautiful Shadows

## Use When
- A surface needs polished, layered elevation.
- Default Tailwind shadows feel too generic or blunt.
- The design needs neutral, refined depth without colored glow.

## Shadow Utilities
Use these exact Tailwind classes.

### Beautiful sm
Use for compact cards, form controls, pills, and quieter surfaces.

```txt
shadow-[0px_2px_3px_-1px_rgba(0,0,0,0.1),0px_1px_0px_0px_rgba(25,28,33,0.02),0px_0px_0px_1px_rgba(25,28,33,0.08)]
```

### Beautiful md
Use for cards, panels, popovers, and the default elevated surface style.

```txt
shadow-[0px_0px_0px_1px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_1px_1px_-0.5px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_3px_3px_-1.5px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),_0px_6px_6px_-3px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_12px_12px_-6px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_24px_24px_-12px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06)]
```

### Beautiful lg
Use for hero media, feature callouts, modal-like containers, and the strongest lift.

```txt
shadow-[0_2.8px_2.2px_rgba(0,_0,_0,_0.034),_0_6.7px_5.3px_rgba(0,_0,_0,_0.048),_0_12.5px_10px_rgba(0,_0,_0,_0.06),_0_22.3px_17.9px_rgba(0,_0,_0,_0.072),_0_41.8px_33.4px_rgba(0,_0,_0,_0.086),_0_100px_80px_rgba(0,_0,_0,_0.12)]
```

## Examples

```html
<div class="rounded-xl bg-white shadow-[0px_0px_0px_1px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_1px_1px_-0.5px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_3px_3px_-1.5px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),_0px_6px_6px_-3px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_12px_12px_-6px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06),0px_24px_24px_-12px_rgba(0,0,0,0.06)]">
  ...
</div>
```

## Usage Rules
- Use `Beautiful sm` for compact cards, form controls, pills, and quieter surfaces.
- Use `Beautiful md` for cards, panels, popovers, and the default elevated surface style.
- Use `Beautiful lg` for hero media, feature callouts, modal-like containers, and the strongest lift.
- Pair with a clean surface fill and a consistent radius.
- Use one shadow strength per component state unless an interaction clearly changes elevation.

## Avoid
- Mixing these with default Tailwind shadow scales on the same component.
- Tinting these shadows with strong colors; keep them neutral and refined.
- Applying `Beautiful lg` to dense lists or tiny controls.
- Stacking multiple shadow utilities on one element.
- Using these as a substitute for clear borders in very low-contrast layouts.

</skill>
